# Decisions, and the single list of what is still open

**This document replaces §3 and §4 of the mega handoff and the scattered open-item lists in the
session handoffs and the design ledger.** It is the only place either list lives now.

Commit references are pointers into history, not the current head. **A decision marked superseded
keeps its row, because in each of those cases the mistake is the lesson** — but only the
superseding decision describes the code as it stands.

---

## Part 1 — The decision log

### Signal processing and the conversion to device units

| # | Decision | Why | Ref |
|---|---|---|---|
| 13 | **The 60 Hz mains notch is off by default.** | The device is implanted and battery-powered, so there is no mains coupling, and notching removes real signal. The option is retained. | `f915257` |
| 14 | **Only the two 256-point modes may be converted** — Dual and Single Inverse. | A 64-point spectrum integrates a different set of frequency bins, so it is a different quantity and the same gain cannot convert it. | `f915257` |
| 15 | **Converting a spectrum back to a time series and forward again adds nothing.** The direct route from spectrum to device units is the rigorous one. | A phase-randomised reconstruction matches the direct band integral within 0.8 percent, median ratio 1.008, r = 0.999, on all 113 paired blocks. A band integral is phase-independent. | `f915257` |
| 18 | **The primary conversion from the voltage trace is the transform route at 352.62.** | It reaches r = 0.9927 and a root-mean-square error of 60.6 device units. The Welch route produces a spectrum from the voltage trace and cannot consume the device's own spectrum, so it was never a valid substitute when no voltage trace exists. | CS-1 |
| 4 | **Windows of the voltage trace with more than 10 percent missing samples are dropped entirely** rather than analysed. | The zero-fill from the concatenation repair entered the spectrum as real zeros and deflated band power. | `adcaf15`, `c438ce1` |
| 3 | **The concatenation repair is left in place**; matching is unchanged. | Re-decoded both ways: 67 matched against 67, zero changes. | no change |
| 2 | **The pain-report timestamp is California local wall-clock time and must be converted.** | Device start times are already universal, so parsing the report as universal smears every match by seven to eight hours. | `a4e4e68` |

### The frozen per-participant conversion model

| # | Decision | Why | Ref |
|---|---|---|---|
| 11 | **Freeze the per-participant model as a stored asset**, with a tiered fallback from band to channel-and-frequency to channel-pooled to none, and mark a modelled switching value as indeterminate. | The device reports its own units while the offline route reports microvolts squared, and a band the device never sensed needs an estimated switching value that is clearly flagged as estimated. | `771f3c2` |
| 16 | **The 8.8 Hz band on the right 0-3 contact is restricted to on or after 2026-03-01**, not the configuration-change date of 2025-12-05. | The configuration-change date sits inside a declining settling transient, −0.078 per month in the logarithm, p = 0.039, with stationarity only from about 2026-02-15. Using the earlier date would inject the higher-gain transient and bias the deployable switching value. The frozen fit is unchanged. | `e9d7a80` |
| 17 | **An impedance term of 1.02 is rejected.** | Significant only under a naive fit, because 2,985 measurements share 230 impedance readings. With a grouping-aware standard error it is not significant, p = 0.26. It is a slow-time proxy rather than a physical gain, and its effect on the switching value, 1.22-fold, is smaller than the model's own spread. | `a9c3a01` |

### How results are reported, and the gates

| # | Decision | Why | Ref |
|---|---|---|---|
| 7 | **The interval on a discrimination value is not folded** — the bootstrap keeps the signed value. | Folding hid null bands, and an honest lower bound must be allowed to fall below 0.5. | `c50be37` |
| 8 | **A per-epoch discrimination value is signed, and the orientation is fixed once on the pooled result** rather than per epoch. | A folded per-epoch value masks a sign reversal inside an epoch, which is a closed-loop failure mode. | PR #5 |
| 9 | **Deployment gates have three states, and the stability gate abstains when its test cannot run.** | **A gate that goes green on absence of evidence is unsafe for a claim that something is ready to program.** | PR #5 |
| 12 | **Validation is forward-chaining and out of sample**, with the switching value fitted on the training part only and the held-out value not re-folded. | The in-sample value masked forward reversals: 26.4 Hz went from 0.55 to 0.24, and 8.8 Hz from 0.52 to 0.37. | `d9d58a4` |
| 19 | **Intervals account for autocorrelated ratings** through a moving-block bootstrap and an effective sample size, with the de-folded lower bound as the gate. | Honest intervals under autocorrelated ratings, and it stops the beats-chance requirement from being re-manufactured by a bias-corrected interval. | `8509e96`, `2ef0408`, `abe8a23` |
| 6 | **The net-benefit cut-point rule was removed.** | Its objective equals the prevalence multiplied by the cost, so it always picks the identical point as the cost rule — two controls that cannot disagree. | PR #3 |
| 10 | **The recommendation is single-sourced**, with an explicit difference between what is recommended and what is currently programmed, and conservative abstention on ramp guidance. | An actionable recommendation while keeping the fail-closed discipline. | `b0597f8` |

### The deployment fallback, and the units error behind its change

| # | Decision | Why | Ref |
|---|---|---|---|
| ~~20~~ | ~~**The deployment fallback converts the cut-point through the frozen model**, and fails closed when no fitted entry exists.~~ **SUPERSEDED BY 21.** | The reasoning was that the cut-point's own model should convert it. **The lesson kept from this row is the units error that followed.** | `184ea74`, `fa2c416` |
| 21 | **The deployment fallback models the device's power line off the raw voltage trace at the cut-point's own centre frequency, and anchors by rank.** The switching value is never converted. | **Decision 20 was a units error: the cut-point is a z-scored logarithm of power, not a linear microvolts squared, so feeding it to the frozen model mis-read every value — a z at or below zero came out as a device reading near zero.** The new path honours the centre exactly with no snapping, fails closed when neither route is available, and leaves the exploration timeline unchanged. | `09798f7` |

### Platform and interface

| # | Decision | Why | Ref |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **The R interface converter is constructed without the frame converter.** | The frame converter turned a control list into a form the R bridge cannot hand back to the model fitter. | `33f45a5` |
| 5 | **The closed-loop figures draw once and mutate by restyling a trace index**, with the request parameters memoised and a permanent drawing element. | A new object identity on every render refetched all four panels, so every figure flashed back to its loading state. **This is a hard constraint** — rebuilding a figure on interaction reintroduces it. | `255e0ef` |

### The cache store, decided 2026-09-06 and 2026-09-07

| # | Decision | Why | When |
|---|---|---|---|
| ~~22~~ | ~~**The recordings may be cached with no expiry; the pain reports may not be cached at all.**~~ **AMENDED BY 78** — the recordings half stands unchanged; the "not cached at all" half now has one carved-out exception, the read-only drill-downs. | The recordings have a freshness check cheaper than the work, built from values already in hand. The pain reports do not — measured twice, the cheapest check costs the same as an outright fresh fetch. **That measurement is still correct and decision 78 does not claim otherwise: it buys speed by accepting a bounded staleness, not by finding a cheap freshness check.** | 2026-09-06 |
| 23 | **No pain rating enters the tile file or its key.** | The tiles know nothing about any rating and the same tiles serve every pain score, match rule and length of signal. Keying on the report set would discard a 37-second build every time a report was filed. Proven both ways: changing the report set changes 19,464 of 27,305 answer values **and causes zero writes.** | 2026-09-06 |
| 24 | **The file key is built from the database rows alone, never from the decoded recordings.** | The point of a file is to be found **before** decoding; the warming path especially must answer "is this built?" without opening 569 files. Costs 0.33 s for 4,078 rows. | 2026-09-06 |
| 25 | **The key carries the patient-event row metadata, the decode-time stamping, and every constant the stored numbers depend on.** | Patient-event rows have no content hash at all — 0 of 3,246 — because their spectra are the metadata. The sensing centre and schedules can change with no content change. And a file outlives the process that wrote it. | 2026-09-06 |
| 26 | **The key decides whether to write, not the caller.** A page whose key matches never writes, and that is asserted by test. | Adopted over the first proposal that ingestion be the only writer, which would have left every page slow until each participant's next upload, because the key contains code-version values so a deployment invalidates everything. | 2026-09-06 |
| 27 | **Redis is scoped to build locks, freshness keys and Django's small cache values. The large stored products stay as files.** | Measured: the 245.90 MB tile store reads in 0.05 s from the filesystem against about 0.14 s through Redis, roughly three times slower, because the files are already in the page cache. **The problem is computation, not latency.** | 2026-09-06 |
| 28 | **Redis is bounded at 512 MB with least-recently-used eviction across all keys**, set live and durably on the redis service in both compose files. | It had been unlimited with eviction disabled, which means growing until the host fills and then **refusing new writes** — the opposite of cache behaviour, on a box shared with the database and four workers. Across all keys rather than only expiring ones, because one key written without an expiry could restore that wall. **Authorised out of the plan's order because it was a live hazard.** | 2026-09-06, `b7036bf` |
| 29 | **Stored tables use Parquet with zstd; the spectrum arrays stay as compressed array files.** | Chosen for size and durability, seventeen times smaller than the alternative, and a stable published format. **Pickle writes twice as fast and reads are tied — that caveat travels with the choice.** Comma-separated and JSON files are excluded **on correctness**: the therapy table carries a timezone-aware timestamp and both lose it. | 2026-09-06 |
| 30 | **One store implementation as a superset callable by any module; the duplicate is deleted.** | Two implementations exist with their own resolver, loader, writer, counters and lock, sharing a root by construction accident, with per-entry limits differing by exactly a factor of four for no stated reason. | 2026-09-07 |
| 31 | **The provenance chain and its proven refusal come before anything that writes back to the store.** The refusal is proven by constructing a deliberate cycle. | The four arrows let Stim Optimizer read a verdict computed from recordings Stim Optimizer chose to collect. **Nothing crashes — the exploration policy becomes self-confirming and the record looks like converging evidence when it is a loop.** | 2026-09-07 |
| 32 | **The band range is all 22 centres from 8.5 to 29.5 Hz.** | The PI's flowchart said "8-20" and he corrected it: *"I meant all the bands that this week already covers."* Taking 8 to 20 Hz literally would have dropped the 22 to 27 Hz range holding this record's clearest amplitude response. | 2026-09-07 |
| 33 | **The ground-truth rule for the three-source comparison** — the device's own band power where it exists **and passes a per-channel saturation ceiling check**; the calibrated voltage-trace route where it does not; the composed device-spectrum route below that and tagged composed; never the uncalibrated integrated-density route. Where both exist for one band and setting, **both values and the fold ratio between them are written**. Every row carries its route, its window count, and whether the band sits inside the checked conversion span. | The device's own reading is the exact quantity the control law compares against a switching value typed in those units. **The ceiling check is required because about 1 percent of simultaneous windows are device-side spikes, and a rule adopting the unfiltered reading adopts those spikes as truth.** The fold ratio is the only continuous check on whether the calibration serving the other 97 bands still holds. | 2026-09-07 |
| 34 | **The written record is consolidated into five reference documents; the 50 superseded documents are moved to an archive with `git mv`, never deleted.** No suite count is written into any of them. | *"There are too many Handoffs going on now."* Counts in those documents went stale within single sessions and one reached a pushed commit message that cannot be edited. | 2026-09-07 |
| 35 | **The pain-report snapshot (Track A step 4) is keyed on the content of the tidy table alone, is read by no page, and the store keeps that kind's history rather than sweeping it.** Decision 22 stands: the reports are fetched fresh on every request. | The stored copy buys reproducibility and a key for derived products, not speed. The same table requested two ways is one report set. A swept snapshot would leave the ledger row and not the table, which is the one thing an audit needs; each is about 28 KB, one per distinct report set. Proven on the live record: 9,120 fields compared between the stored and the fetched table, 0 differences; a second fetch leaves the directory byte-identical. | 2026-09-07 |
| 36 | **The project's plan is kept with the `planning-with-files` plugin, in its parseable shape, in legacy mode with structure-aware injection and without attestation or a completion gate; the planning files stay committed.** `CLAUDE.md` §4 carries the rules. | The plugin's completion check read nothing from the earlier heading shape. Attestation blocks context injection whenever the plan file changes until it is re-attested, and the plan changes after every phase. The gate can refuse a stop, and here the PI decides when work stops. Committing keeps the plan with the code it describes. | 2026-09-07 |
| 37 | **Track A step 5: the settings stream is stored as the raw kind `therapy_settings`, keyed on the participant's source-file rows (uid, content hash, type; never the file name), and the epoch-level matched table as `therapy_pain_matched`, keyed on the settings key and the pain-report snapshot key with both in its provenance; `therapy_pain_matched` is registered as raw-derived.** An empty stream, a stream with unreadable files, or a table whose inputs cannot both be named is handed back and never stored. | The stream costs about 33 s to parse and its file rows identify it before anything is decoded (decision 24); a file name can carry a patient's name. The report key is what makes a newly filed report a new entry. The matched table is a deterministic join of two raw inputs and embodies no exploration choice, so refusing it to Stim Optimizer would refuse the table the step exists to give it; the ladder it chooses stays a derived kind and is still refused. Proven on RCS08: 59,661 fields compared between the stored and the freshly parsed stream in each of three alternating rounds, 0 differences; stored read 0.009 to 0.013 s against 32.56 to 33.61 s to parse; the matched table 3,036 fields, 0 differences. | 2026-09-07 |
| 38 | **Track A step 6: the band-by-length sweep writes back two tidy tables, `biomarker_band_correlation` and `biomarker_band_discrimination` (one row per contact pair, band centre and length of signal, every value copied from the response, the best row per centre flagged with its interval and verdict, the no-relationship reference on every row: 0 for a correlation and 0.5 for an area under the curve), and the response itself as `biomarker_band_sweep`, all three under one key naming the tile entry, the pain-report snapshot, the pain score and every setting; a request whose key matches is served from the store and says so.** The store is asked before the spectra, the event blocks and the tile cache are loaded. | The approved plan asks for the two tables. Serving the response follows decision 26: every input that could change the answer is in the key, and the thousand shuffles and resamples per cell are not paid again when nothing changed. Proven on RCS08, three alternating rounds: 27,311 response values compared, 0 differences each round; both tables 1,320 rows with 0 differences against the response grids; 22 centres, 8.5 to 29.5 Hz. Fresh 6.17, 5.65 and 5.52 s against served 2.59, 3.80 and 2.50 s; what a served request still pays is the report fetch and the time-domain recording load. **The served-response part goes beyond step 6's wording and is named for the PI's sign-off** (`artifacts/review_2026-09-07_PS_closedloop_deployment.md`). | 2026-09-07 |
| 39 | **The store refuses a derived kind written with no writer, counts a derived kind written with no provenance chain, records the ledger for production-root writes only, and resolves both of its import spellings to one module object.** The future chosen-ladder kind is `exploration_ladder`, not `settings_stream`. | From the six-perspective review of the branch on 2026-09-07. A sidecar with no writer and no chain looks like a raw input to anything that cites it. The live ledger held 218 test-written rows out of 227, deleted the same day. Two spellings had become two module objects in one process, so a test's outcome depended on the order of the suites on the command line. The kind name collided with the function that reads the device's programmed history, a raw thing. | 2026-09-07 |
| 48 | **Track C step 4: every module response carries `cache_status` (whether a stored entry exists under the current key, its build date from the entry's own sidecar, the trigger, a sentence saying what the date means on that page) and one shared line under each page's recompute control shows it, including "no stored results yet".** | The plan asks for the date on all three pages including the no-cache case; the three dates mean different things, so each page's sentence says which. Read from the sidecar, never a file timestamp. Live on RCS08 all three answer in under half a second. | 2026-09-07 |

| ~~50~~ | ~~Track E: the assembled spectrum matrix and the per-recording spectrum files both move into the one store as raw kinds; the per-recording directory is deleted rather than migrated.~~ **SUPERSEDED BY 51.** | The reasoning was that the per-recording directory was read by no page request. **The lesson kept from this row: "read by no page" was true and beside the point** — the directory's value is in an automatic background step and a request handler, neither of which is a page GET, and both of which pay a real, recurring cost without it. | 2026-09-07 |
| 51 | **Track E, revised: the assembled matrix still moves into the one store as a raw kind (not yet built). The per-recording directory stays exactly where it is, sped up in front rather than replaced**, with a stamp over the whole recording set that skips the per-recording loop entirely when nothing has moved, and a small saved list that replaces asking the file system about every recording individually when something has.** | Measured with alternating rounds: removing the directory would cost about 2.5-3.1 s on every future upload and every band-conversion panel view, not once but every time, forever. Moving it into the one store instead would force a full rewrite on every new recording (the store keeps one current snapshot per participant per kind) or a database row and a small file for each of about 6,300 recordings — neither is the store's job. Proven on RCS08: 1,149,923 fields compared between the per-recording loop and the new stamp-served rows, 0 differences; a cache hit against a full recompute, twice each in alternating order, 0.355 s / 0.351 s against 3.138 s / 2.909 s. A first draft would have redecoded a participant's whole history the first time it ran; caught and fixed before shipping. | 2026-09-07 |
| 52 | **The device route's ceiling (open item 20) is a table of precomputed 99.5th-percentile thresholds, one per (electrode, band centre), built once from RCS08's full recorded history rather than from the window being checked — and, separately, the same treatment computed but not yet wired in for the two offline tile families (time-domain-derived and PSD-derived).** His decision on 2026-09-08, after seeing that a same-window fold rule cannot tell a genuinely bad window from a clean one (the "masking" problem) and after a literature review found real precedent for a history-derived reference in the project's own prior work (Prosky et al. 2021) and in Percept-specific literature (Cascino et al. 2026). | The 99.5th percentile discards the top 0.5% of all historical readings per group; a group whose entire history is exactly zero is left out of the table rather than given a ceiling of zero. `CEILING_RULE_VERSION` replaces `DEVICE_SPIKE_FOLD` in the ground-truth signature, so old cache entries built under the fold rule are never served as if built under this one. Proven live on RCS08: all 19 live lookups in the current comparison hit the table correctly; on constructed data, a window that is 60% spikes — which the old rule could not detect at all, because its own median was itself a spike — is now correctly found and excluded. Both suites green: host 936 passed (2 new tests), container 540 unaffected. | 2026-09-08 |

Proven on RCS08: a store round trip (write, then read) is byte-identical, 0 of 654,146 fields differ. A separate check comparing a build made mostly from the per-recording cache against a fully fresh rebuild found 206,107 apparent differences out of 654,146 — traced to a pre-existing property of the row-assembly step this change does not touch, that its row order follows collection order rather than a sorted one; after sorting both to the same order by time, electrode and source, all 629,129 power values and every row's identity matched exactly. Host suite 936 passed, both orders; container suite 540, unaffected. | 2026-09-08 |
| 54 | **The shared result-cache's flat entry-count cap is replaced with a byte budget and a cap on how many distinct participants are kept resident, and the do-not-edit restriction on the three files that hold it is lifted for this one change.** | A count was always the wrong unit for entries three orders of magnitude apart in size (twenty kilobytes to nineteen megabytes), and the count also conflated two different problems: one participant's own module fan-out (up to nine slots) and unbounded growth across many participants in one session. The two new bounds separate them; the heap-pressure guard remains the actual safety net either way. | 2026-09-08 |
| 55 | **A switching value may be placed on a band whose power rises then falls with current, but only on the side of the peak where one power value maps to one current. The current-to-power relationship is tested for curvature first, pooled across every stimulation-current ladder this participant has across every visit rather than one visit at a time, with the grouping between visits accounted for rather than pooled points treated as independent. When a real bend is confirmed and fits well, a second straight-line fit is added using only the data from the peak current onward.** | The device places its switching value between two power readings; a peaked band satisfies the same value going up and coming down, so the device cannot tell the two situations apart from the power reading alone unless the switching value sits on the one-sided, unambiguous stretch. Pooling across visits also answers open item 18: a single visit's ladder has too few settled points for the curvature test, but combined ladders across visits should clear it. | 2026-09-08 |
| 56 | **Built and proven: the pooled, cluster-robust curvature test (`within_visit.amplitude_response_shape_pooled`) confirmed live on RCS08 rather than only on constructed data.** Pooling across visits does clear the eight-point floor for two of the three contacts with any coverage at 25-28 Hz: ONE_THREE_LEFT reaches 13 points across 4 visits, ZERO_THREE_RIGHT reaches 12 across 6. The result is more mixed than the single-day clinic-sheet finding: on ONE_THREE_LEFT, 26.5 and 27.5 Hz show significant curvature but without a clean peak inside the tested currents, while 25.5 and 28.5 Hz show none; on ZERO_THREE_RIGHT none of the four bands show curvature; ZERO_TWO_LEFT still has only one visit's worth of data (5 points) regardless of pooling. | On constructed data: a fixture built to fake a peak purely from between-visit baseline differences is correctly NOT reported as curved once visit is accounted for (the naive, unpooled test on the same points is fooled by it, confirming the fixture does what it claims); a genuine peak riding on three different per-visit baselines is correctly detected with the peak location undistorted. Host suite 940 passed (was 936, +4 new tests), both orders. | 2026-09-08 |
| 57 | **The review authorized by item 6 found one confirmed, currently-live correctness gap in the entangled spectrum-and-pain routine, `streaming_psd.compute_psd_pain_correlation`: it never applies the zero-fill rejection rule (decision 4) that the rest of the pipeline has enforced since June, because the adapter that feeds it windows drops the field that carries how much of each window was filled in.** No fix has been made — see open item 9 for the three-part recommendation and why his go-ahead is needed first. Two smaller things the review found and corrected in its own draft before reporting them: only one production call site reaches this routine, not two as first read, because a second apparent site calls a different module's function of the same name; and two existing tests do exercise this routine's output live on RCS08, but only for internal self-consistency, never against a fixed known-correct value — the earlier claim that no test touches it at all was too broad and is withdrawn. | A same-window rejection rule with no visible failure mode is exactly the kind of gap that survives unnoticed (§2 principle 2's own reasoning for why this project treats a false-passing test as worse than none): every number this routine has ever produced from a heavily filled-in window is quietly wrong in the same silent way, and nothing before this review checked whether it was even applying the rule. | 2026-09-08 |

Parts (2) (folding this routine into the newer sweep) and (3) (leaving the statistics alone) are unchanged from decision 57/item 9's recommendation; part (3) still stands — no statistical method was touched. | 2026-09-08 |
| 59 | **Proven on live RCS08 data, both directions (his own choice: fix now, measure first): of the 386 recordings this routine analyses, 31 (8%) are more than 10% zero-filled in their first 30 seconds and are now correctly excluded instead of pooled; 87 carry some zero-fill at all, from 0.16% up to 61.6% of one recording. Comparing the full page response before and after: 8,097,776 fields before, 8,097,533 after; of the 8,097,533 fields both responses have in common, 29,704 changed value and 243 dropped out entirely (shorter derived scatter-overlay lists, not missing data). For at least one channel, the routine's own choice of "best" frequency band moved, from 3.9215 Hz to 0.95 Hz, because the contaminated recordings no longer count toward that choice.** | This is a field-count and difference-count proof, not a tolerance, on the live participant, exactly as this project's rule requires for any change to a stored or served number (§2 principle 2, `ARCHITECTURE_cache_store.md` §7). The band-selection shift is reported because it is the kind of change a reader of the Biomarkers page would notice and should be told about, not smoothed into an aggregate percentage. | 2026-09-08 |
| 60 | **The Recompute double-fetch question (open item 21, decision 54) is closed: watched live in a browser rather than concluded from code alone.** No shared login exists for this local instance (checked: no seeded `PlatformUser`, no fixture, no documented dev credential), so a throwaway account was self-registered through the app's own open sign-up form, and a synthetic participant with no real data was created under it — sufficient because the question is about the client's request-counting logic, not about any patient's data. Pressing Recompute on that participant's Biomarkers page fired exactly one `queryBiomarkerAnalysis` request, confirmed again after a further wait. The throwaway participant was deleted afterward through the app's own delete confirmation; the throwaway login was left in place (harmless, no data access) rather than hunting for an account-deletion path that does not obviously exist. The Closed-Loop Deployment page's own Recompute path needs a committed band, which needs real recordings this synthetic participant does not have, so it was not separately click-tested; it shares the same underlying hook (`useCachedResult.js`) this check exercised. | Item 21 was reasoned from reading `useCachedResult.js` but not watched happen, and a prior attempt was blocked by the same login wall this session hit first. §2 principle 2 treats an unwatched conclusion as unproven; this closes it with an observed count rather than a repeated hunch. | 2026-09-08 |
| 61 | **Open item 22 (folding the entangled routine's calculation into the newer band-by-length sweep) is resolved as: do not fold.** The channel-set check the item asked for does pass — both routines derive their contact list from the identical function (`_derive_chan_order`) over the identical loader call, on the identical raw recordings, so they look at the same electrode contacts. But that was never the real obstacle. The two routines differ on four independent axes: the entangled routine correlates raw, uncalibrated Welch power over a continuous 0.95-100 Hz spectrum, one epoch per streaming session, with an FDR-and-cluster-robust-corrected analytic p-value; the sweep correlates calibrated device-LSB power over 22 fixed 8.5-29.5 Hz centres crossed with ten window lengths, one row per pain report, with a permutation-and-bootstrap-corrected p-value and an AUC statistic the entangled routine does not compute at all. A literal fold would silently change the reported scale, the sample unit, the frequency coverage, and the correction method all at once. | Decision 57/58's own text only ever hedged on the channel-set question and never asserted the two calculations were otherwise the same; nothing in `DESIGN_biomarker_pipeline_v2.md` or `ARCHITECTURE_modules_and_store.md` claims a shared core exists to fold into. Keeping them separate is the scientifically correct state, not an unfinished merge — recorded here so open item 22 is not periodically re-opened as if a fold were merely undone work. | 2026-09-08 |

port, not merely a config-vectorised replay run 200 times.** The controller resets its state at the start of every stretch (confirmed by reading `run_stretch`'s own body: `amp = np.full(K, amp_init)`, `adopted = np.full(K, BETWEEN)`), so one stretch's outcome never depends on another's and is deterministic given a stretch and a configuration — which means a block bootstrap's answer, a weighted sum over whichever stretches a resample draws with replacement, can be computed from numbers worked out ONCE per training stretch rather than re-simulated per replicate. `_stretch_accumulators` runs the one unavoidable per-reading, config-vectorised replay loop exactly once over the training stretches; every one of the 200 bootstrap replicates is then pure matrix arithmetic agree to 1e-9 including when a resample draws one stretch twice and leaves two out. **Batching the 200 replicates into the configuration axis too (the "even better" option named in the brief) was | 210 | **The one-band rule's pain leg is loosened from "established" to "supported": a band counts when its best-of-lengths correlation with the pain score is positive AND its own block-bootstrap interval lies wholly above zero; clearing the grid's extra selection-aware bar ("established") is reported beside it, not required -- the PI, 2026-09-20: "Loosen the rule for the stim optimizer so at least some bands pass ... start with the most scientifically valid ones".** WHY THIS LOOSENING AND NOT ANOTHER. The grid's "established" asks two things of a band: that its interval (decision 183's block bootstrap) lie wholly off zero, and that the value beat what the same best-of-nine-lengths choice reaches on shuffled pain scores. The second guards the choice of length; the first is the classical criterion at the chosen length. The ten pain reports of 2026-09-20 took R 1-3+'s best band from r 0.44 (established, n 77) to 0.23 (n 87): interval 0.05-0.41, still wholly above zero, but under the shuffle bar of 0.28, and the readiness screen went from six usable cells to none. Dropping the selection-aware bar keeps the interval test, and the joint rule still requires the SAME band to fall with current on an independent era-blocked test, so the conjunction stays conservative; a positive point value whose interval includes zero still does not count, and a row with no interval can only count by being established. The joint corners of the session plan (decision 160) are therefore not a garnish, they are the only way the held side's span is ever earned -- and the gap now proposes corner pairs first whenever the other side has not moved, and says why. | 240 | **The two guards every offline model on this record has to pass through, built as shared tools, and the first live run of the one the review panel made a condition on building any decoder.** Step 6 of the cross-cutting build order; panel B items 2 and 7 of decision 232. **(a) Held-out blocks of TIME with the neighbours dropped** (`stats_utils.purged_time_blocked_folds`): the test rows are a contiguous stretch of time, never a scatter of rows, and every training row within the embargo of that stretch is dropped. The embargo is the label's OWN decorrelation timescale (`block_length_for`, the same estimator the permutation nulls already use), never a number anybody chose. Constructed proof, 400 rows at lag-1 0.95, its own timescale 16 rows: a model that does nothing but copy the value of its nearest training row scores 0.83 with the neighbours present and 0.19 with them dropped, in blocks of 10 rows; the same comparison in blocks of 3 rows is 0.92 against 0.22, and in blocks of 80 rows the trick has no neighbours to exploit and both columns are noise (0.18 and 0.33). So the guard bites hardest where the blocks are short, which is what a record this size forces. **(b) The current-confound gate** (`stats_utils.confound_gate`): one candidate reported plainly AND with a third quantity taken out, a bootstrap interval on each, `survives` True / False / None, where None is a covariate that never moves, too few usable rows, or a candidate that is almost a straight line in the covariate -- not a pass and not a failure, and never silently a pass. **(c) The pre-build diagnostic** (`Biomarkers/routines/confound_diagnostic.py`): the pain label read out of sample three ways -- from the stimulation current alone (the floor a decoder has to beat), from every band, and from every band with the current taken out of each one, the straight line for that removal fitted inside the training rows and applied to the held-out ones so the removal cannot leak -- against a rotation null that keeps pain's own day-to-day persistence. A ridge on standardised features, chosen because a reader can follow it. The score is NOT folded: elsewhere in this module an undirected single-band screen reports max(AUC, 1-AUC), which is right there and wrong here, because a fitted model scoring 0.30 out of sample got the direction wrong and printing 0.70 would invert the finding. **[Corrected 2026-09-26, decision 310: every out-of-sample reading here pooled the held-out scores across the blocks of time, which credits whatever moves pain and the bands together between blocks; scored within each block, and at the page's 60-minute matching where 262 moved them, L 1-3+ reads every band 0.570 / 0.589 at 30 / 60 s (shuffled-data 95th 0.615 / 0.654, p 0.159 / 0.189) and the current alone 0.491 / 0.483; no set of bands beats its own null on any pair.]** | The review panel would not let a decoder be built here until one question was answered on the live data: how much of what a decoder could learn is the stimulation current rather than the brain. On this participant the current moves the band power and moves the pain, and the pain scores resemble their neighbours in time, so a model can score well for either reason while carrying no fact about the brain. LIVE ON RCS08 (nrs, tertile split, the page's own matcher and 3 s-piece cache, store writes off), at 1, 10, 30 and 60 s of signal on the two sensing pairs with enough reports: **no set of bands beats its own null out of sample anywhere.** Best: L 1-3+ at 30 s, 0.701 against a null median 0.498 and 95th 0.713, p 0.075 on 53 reports, uncorrected for the eight combinations tried; R 0-3+ at 30 s, 0.396 against a null 95th of 0.622. Taking the current out never lifts a reading above its null (L 1-3+ 30 s: 0.478; R 0-3+ 30 s: 0.579 against 0.622). Every band on both sides together **cannot be read at all**: 46 reports carry all 44 bands, so holding a block of time out leaves fewer training rows than there are bands -- the module prints that sentence instead of a number. The one strong reading is the **stimulation current alone on L 1-3+, 0.879**, and it is an era as much as a dose: 19 of those 53 reports sit at zero current, every one of them between 2025-07-23 and 2025-10-14, mean pain 8.1 against about 6.1 across the later settings; on R 0-3+, where the subset is large enough to test, dropping the zero-current reports takes the current's own out-of-sample skill from 0.411 to 0.242 -- the relationship reverses on data it has not seen. Direction, from the values rather than from a model: current against pain reads -0.507 on the left rows and -0.189 on the right, higher current with lower pain, the same direction as the current-map card's caveat (decision 235) and equally unable to survive out of sample. Nothing here refuses anything (the PI, 2026-09-22: a warning, never blocking) and **none of it is on any page today**; it is the gate the next decoder has to pass through, and the measured ceiling whoever proposes one starts from. | 241 | **The stimulation current is given a SHAPE when it is taken out of something, and the shape is named on every answer with the flexibility it cost.** The PI, 2026-09-22, on decision 240: "I think sometimes we see an upside-down U-shaped curve in the current-pain relationship. So, linear might actually mask a useful measure"; then, having seen the quadratic: model it with two or three other common kernel functions. `stats_utils.CovariateShape` offers seven -- a straight line, a squared term, a 3-knot natural cubic spline (Harrell's restricted form), three kernels fitted by kernel ridge (squared exponential, Matern 3/2, rational quadratic, length scale read off the delivered settings by the median rule, ridge 0.1), and one level per delivered setting. Every answer carries the shape, its effective degrees of freedom (the trace of the smoother: 1 for a line, 2 for the spline, 4-9 for the kernels), the length scale and why it was chosen, and the reason wherever a shape could not be built and something cheaper was used instead; an unknown shape is refused by name. The removal is fitted on the TRAINING rows and applied to the held-out ones, kernels included, and a test scribbles on the held-out rows to prove the training fit does not move. `feature_columns()` writes the same shape as columns for when the current is the thing being read rather than removed, so the floor a decoder must beat is measured as flexibly as the adjustment assumes. **The two new guards default to the spline; `partial_corr` and `partial_corr_columns` keep the straight line**, so decision 234's adjusted grid and every other published number are untouched, and a test pins the line path to the old answer to 1e-12. | A straight line cannot remove an effect that turns over, and what it leaves behind looks like a relationship between the two things it was removed from -- the false positive -- while a candidate whose own relationship curves reads near zero. WHY THE SPLINE IS THE DEFAULT AND A KERNEL IS NOT, measured live before choosing (RCS08, L 1-3+, 53 reports, 11 delivered currents, nrs, 30 s of signal): a straight line explains 25.7% of pain's scatter and is still falling at the highest current tested; the squared term and the spline explain 30.6% and 29.4% and put the lowest pain at 3.11 and 3.21 mA; the squared-exponential, Matern 3/2 and rational-quadratic kernels explain 36.9%, 42.8% and 39.3% for 4.3, 6.2 and 4.8 degrees of freedom and move the lowest pain to 1.81-1.93 mA, chasing two ratings at 1.6 mA (mean pain 3.0, filed 2026-03-31 and 2026-04-16); one level per setting explains 62.0% with 10 degrees of freedom on 53 reports. The flexible shapes do not agree with each other about where the turn is, so the default is the cheapest shape that can turn over at all. The turn-over is suggestive, not established: the squared term is +0.214 with a 95% interval of -0.079 to +0.486. On R 0-3+ (96 reports) there is no turn-over under any shape -- every one puts the lowest pain at the highest current tested, and the line, the squared term and the spline agree to three decimals. WHAT THE SHAPE CHANGES ON THE LIVE RECORD: nothing that matters. Under all seven shapes on both pairs, no set of bands beats its own null out of sample and the adjusted reading stays inside it (L 1-3+ 0.478 line, 0.528 squared, 0.526 spline, 0.535 squared-exponential, 0.559 Matern, 0.545 rational-quadratic, 0.501 per setting, against a null 95th of 0.713; R 0-3+ 0.579, 0.528, 0.524, 0.534, 0.498, 0.527, 0.431 against 0.622). Decision 240's numbers stand as measured with a line and are reported beside the spline's. WHAT THE SHAPE DOES CHANGE, on constructed data where the current turns over: a straight line reads the current itself at 0.622, close to chance -- the answer that would have said the current is not the story here -- while the spline reads the same current at 0.977; and a curved confound left in the bands reads 0.959 after a straight-line removal, 0.570 after the spline and 0.495 after one level per setting. Found while building and pinned as its own test: how much a removal leaves depends on how large the confound is, not only on the shape -- a 3-knot spline fits 99.0% of an exact parabola, and where that parabola is ten times the noise the 1.0% it leaves still correlates two unrelated series at +0.82, which only a shape fitting it exactly clears. So "adjusted" always means "adjusted as well as this shape can", and the size of what was left is part of the answer. | `stats_utils.CovariateShape`, `_shape_in_words`, `partial_corr(shape=)`, `confound_diagnostic.DEFAULT_SHAPE` and `all_bands_auc(shape=)`, `Biomarkers/tests/test_covariate_shape.py` (11 tests, RED first); container 691 -> 702 / 0, host 1429 / 2 / 0 |

Nothing about time enters any model (193-196); this names the movement, it does not model it. The block fold's held-out predictions are now made once and shared by the check and the diagnosis. So, by the panel's own condition, no boundary-avoiding kernel is built: it would answer thin data at the edges, and this surface's problem is that it moves. Every other fitted surface reads "honest but uninformative: thin data" (left leg 110 Hz; the clinic stream at 55 Hz 60/160 and 100/150 and 110 Hz; the back site at 110 Hz): intervals that cover (0.90-1.00) around a surface no better than the mean. | 266 | **Speed-up item 1: the tiles built in batches.** Speed-up item 1: the three-second tiles are built in batches (the PI, 2026-09-25, "go on all", on the handover from the grant session). The tile build called the transform once per 3 s piece (299,593 calls on RCS08) and made one Python row per piece: 25.6 s and 18.2 s of a 47.8 s profiled build. Now the per-piece gates (enough finite signal, no sample at the rail, missing packets within 10%) are computed for every piece of a recording at once, the pieces that pass and are whole and all-finite are transformed together (`analytics.td_transform_band_power_batch`, 4,096 pieces a batch), the rest take the single call, and the rows are made in one pass (`availability._td_tiles_batched`). THE FFT IS STILL CALLED ONCE PER PIECE: the container's FFT library (numpy 2.1.3, aarch64) does rows in pairs and a leftover row alone and rounds the two differently in the last digit (up to 1.6e-14), so batching the FFT across pieces changed values; keeping each piece's own call keeps them identical. Live RCS08, the six sensing pairs, 302,415 tiles: 32,310,237 fields, 0 differing (the build run twice under the old code also differs in 0); alternating rounds before / after / before / after 35.8 / 12.4 / 37.0 / 12.5 s. The saved tiles are the same product, so their key is unchanged. Profiled on the inputs of a live Closed-Loop request (53,246 samples, 30 pain reports): 10.5 s of the step was 1,003 two-column least-squares fits at 9.6 ms each, the container's 16 OpenBLAS threads spending longer coordinating than fitting (40 fits: 0.36 s on 16 threads, 0.03 s on one). The fits' coefficients were identical at 1, 2, 4, 8 and 16 threads, so `_partial_corr_report_bootstrap` caps the library at one thread for its loop (threadpoolctl; without it, as before); nothing else's thread count changes. Batching the fits into one computation was not done: it changes the arithmetic. | 269 | **Speed-up item 3: the design rule's filter compiled, values unchanged.** Speed-up item 3: the design rule's two-component filter runs compiled, every value unchanged (the PI, 2026-09-25: "add numba and do option 1"). numba 0.61.2 and llvmlite 0.44.0 (the line that supports numpy 2.1) are installed in the container and pinned in `requirements.txt`; numpy stays 2.1.3. The filter did about 30 small array operations per time step over 349 stretches x 1,499 steps, 29 ms a call and about 1,600 calls a fit, nearly all numpy's per-call overhead. The compiled loop (`design_rule._filter_2comp_kernel`) performs the same operations on each stretch in the same order: add, subtract, multiply and divide are exactly rounded whichever code runs them; the log is the same library function (2,000 arrays, 0 differing); each step's terms are summed in numpy's own pairwise order over the whole row (3,000 arrays, 0 differing -- a left-to-right sum and "first element plus the rest" both differ). | 289 | **The saved 3-second tiles are read or written only for the one input set every page uses, and nothing before the implant date enters the tiles or the PSDs (the PI, 2026-09-25 night).** WHAT WENT WRONG. The saved tile copy (`raw_lsb_tiles`) was filed under a key built from the participant's database rows, the constants and the band grid -- everything that decides the tiles WHEN the caller hands in the full recording list -- but not from what the caller actually handed in. The Biomarkers page, the Closed-Loop page and the control analyses hand in the time-domain recordings AND the survey/montage recordings (their raw trace is cut into tiles too) and assign each patient-event PSD to a sensing pair through the sensing index; the Stim Optimizer (`_calibrated_lsb_cache`) handed in the 393 time-domain recordings alone and built the patient-event PSDs without the sensing index. Whichever page built first after a daily ingest wrote the copy every page then read. On 2026-09-25 the ingest landed at 19:44 UTC and the Stim Optimizer built first, writing at 20:03:32 UTC a copy of 293,108 TD pieces where the full set is 303,321. The 10,213 missing equal the montage pieces' count. The store's ledger shows the same key written at two sizes twice before: 2026-09-11 20:08 to 09-12 05:15 UTC and 2026-09-17 20:06 to 09-20 08:12 UTC. THE FIX. (a) One helper builds the input set (`_raw_lsb_canonical_inputs`, from `_recordings_setup_cached`, which the heat maps already read) and one asks for the tiles with it (`_raw_lsb_cache_canonical`); the Stim Optimizer and the post-ingest warm-up call it. (b) A request whose own input digest is not the canonical one builds in memory and neither reads nor writes the saved copy; it is counted (`not_the_one_input_set`) and logged, and a failure to read the canonical set costs a build in memory, never a wrong saved copy. (c) The time-domain recordings are labelled inside the tile request, whoever the caller, and a survey/montage recording is never labelled streaming. (d) A 3 s piece whose first sample is before the implant date, and a PSD before it, is dropped on the product (`_cut_before_start`); the implant date is in the saved copy's key (tile rule v2). (e) The Closed-Loop `inputs` key carries the tile key, so its evidence frame rebuilds when the tiles do. | Live RCS08, 2026-09-26, through the bridge: the saved copy under the live key was written at 03:35:16 UTC (ledger key f1d6235721, 249,916,501 bytes; rule `v3_montage_pieces_labelled_montage`, 299) and holds 303,321 TD pieces -- L 1-3+ 52,802, R 1-3+ 45,542, L 0-3+ 47,185, R 0-3+ 65,752, L 0-2+ 46,381, R 0-2+ 45,659; by label 261,636 indefinite stream, 31,472 BrainSense streaming, 10,213 montage -- and 6,075 PSDs (4,512 patient-event, 1,563 montage), 0 of either before 2025-07-16 18:06:21 UTC. A build in memory with the cut switched off gives the same 303,321 and 6,075, so on RCS08 today the cut removes nothing (260 had already dropped the pre-implant patient events where they are read); it stands as a guard. At the page's 60-minute NRS matching (the rule 262 counted): L 1-3+ 201 ratings at 30 s and 187 at 60 s, R 0-3+ 459 and 439; on the short copy, as its band-detector runs of 02:11 UTC recorded, 161 / 108 and 304 / 205. The titration-ladder points were rebuilt from the full tiles at 03:41:58 UTC (8,353 rows). WHAT STANDS AND WHAT DOES NOT: a before/after proof made tonight on the short copy stands, both sides having read the same tiles; an absolute number from it does not, and each one is re-measured in 290-301 or corrected where it was written (P-19, P-12, 285); the earlier windows are listed in Part 2 of the digest. | 290 | **E2 read the ratings of the settings period BEFORE each piece's own, from the day the module was built until 2026-09-25; it reads the piece's own period now.** The Closed-Loop joined table attaches each settings period's pain ratings to the 3 s pieces recorded in it. Live RCS08, `current_explains` run 2026-09-26 08:40:40Z (the committed code on today's data reproduces the saved 2026-09-25 10:36Z run: 144 fields, 0 differing, so every change is the scoring): L 1-3+ 60 s (187 ratings) every band 0.743 -> 0.589 (p 0.040 -> 0.189), with the current taken out 0.683 -> 0.560 (p 0.030 -> 0.184), the current alone 0.708 -> 0.483; 30 s (201) 0.722 -> 0.570 (p 0.035 -> 0.159), 0.681 -> 0.542 (p 0.035 -> 0.239), 0.600 -> 0.491; R 0-3+ 30 s (459), which read BELOW chance pooled, 0.445 -> 0.553 (p 0.801 -> 0.134), the current alone 0.357 -> 0.468. (b) Leaving the identity (a shift of 0) out of the shared rotation null was examined and NOT done, because it makes p too small: with the identity drawn about once in every n draws the observed order is exchangeable with its draws and the p estimates the exact rotation p (smallest 1/n); without it, a band that beats the other n - 1 rotations reads 1/(draws + 1). Under a true null (3,000 records each), p <= 0.01 happened 0.0000 of the time with the identity and 0.030 without at 40 ratings and 1,000 draws, 0.0007 and 0.0133 at 72 ratings; p <= 0.05, 0.046 and 0.069 at 40. A test pins it; the docstring's 'doubly conservative' is corrected (only the +1 is extra). The band detector (297) leaves the identity out in its own rotations: its p-values are too small on average by the same mechanism -- for the PI, not changed; enumerating the n rotations once each would remove the Monte Carlo noise its docstring describes without the bias. So no rotation-null caller changed: live, store writes blocked, committed tree against this one, the heat-map grid (daily defaults) 43,685 fields in common, 19 differing, all timing, 0 added, 0 removed (no p, q or 'clears the correction' value moved; no grid rule bump); the Closed-Loop report L 1-3+ 24.5 Hz 73,450 in common, 0 values differing (2 NaN fields compare unequal to themselves); the band detector, both versions, 4,089 fields, 0 differing. | 199 | **The readiness screen and the two-stage gate call a sensing contact and stimulation rate usable for closed loop when at least ONE band both FALLS with stimulation current once the time confound is removed AND RISES with pain on the stored Biomarkers grid; the two majority rules of decision 143 (half the bands responding on capture, half carrying the negative slope) are gone.** The PI, 2026-09-17, asked why L 1-3+ at 55 Hz was not a sensing option on the Stim Optimizer page; the cause was the 50% rule, written 2026-09-12 (review S4, decision 143; the "correlated family" argument of commit 48bf7abf, first applied 2026-09-02 in 969534d3) -- that contact had 18 of 18 bands falling with current and 0 of 18 clearing the capture-separation floor. His ruling, verbatim: "'at least 50% of the scanned bands respond' doesn't need to be true. We literally only need one actual band that meets the criteria. Those contact and rate combinations should be chosen as responsive and displayed on the Stim Optimizer screen." The two signs together are the device's fixed control polarity (D19, decision 134): more current, less power, less pain. Built: `lfp_evidence.cell_response_verdict(results, pain_positive_centers=)` is THE rule, shared by `screen_cells` and the gate's `check_adaptive_band` (both take `pain_positive_by_channel`); the pain half comes from the stored Biomarkers grid read as this module's own consumer (`ClosedLoopDeployment.adapter.band_sweep_grid_for_closed_loop(..., consumer="stim_optimizer")`, the default NRS grid the daily pass stores), a band counting when its best-of-lengths correlation with the score is positive AND the grid calls it established (`routines/pain_relationship.py`); a contact the grid does not carry leaves its cells NOT ASSESSED, never passed (decision 9); the grid entry's key is in the response key and its chain in the response's provenance. | 193 | **The age penalty in the Stim Optimizer's noise model is inert on this record and rests on no evidence; log drift and raw-unit drift are different things and the one cited was a different quantity.** The PI, 2026-09-17: "where did you get the idea/evidence for penalizing older data?" and "log-scale drift ≠ raw device-unit drift; emphasize/verify this distinction and show me (b)". The term (`objective.observation_variance`, 0.25 x (age in years)²) was written on 2026-08-30 as "the interim stand-in for nonstationarity; the time-varying kernel is deferred" -- a placeholder, no paper, no fit. Decision 16's drift (-0.078/month in the LOG of 8.8 Hz band power on R 0-3+) is a different quantity in different units and was never evidence for it. Re-measured on the same chronic samples: log slope +0.206/month (SE 0.016, +23 %/month multiplicative) against a raw slope of +33,117 device units/month (SE 31,587, indistinguishable from zero) -- raw band power is heavy-tailed (October mean 74,691 against a median of 1,475), so the log slope follows the bulk and the raw slope follows spikes; a device threshold sits on the median. The pain rating itself drifts in raw units: -0.15 points/month on the 0-10 scale (SE 0.03) over 75 epochs and 11.5 months. **(b), the hold-out fit**: Stage 1 fitted on epochs older than 90/120/180 days, the newer epochs predicted at their own settings, `c_age` 0 to 4: the error is identical to three decimals at every weight (RMSE 1.33 / 1.40 / 1.82 on 0-10) -- the term cannot be fitted because it changes nothing; the model over-predicts recent pain by +1.2 to +1.7 points at every cutoff (it does not follow the fall), and for the 90-day hold-out the plain mean of the previous 90 days beats the surface (0.92 against 1.33). A variance penalty cannot correct a bias. Recommended, his call: drop the term; handle drift as a fitted time input if at all. Also fixed in this commit: the current map's colour bar says the rating is on the 0-10 scale (a VAS is divided by ten before the fit). | 154 | **T6 of the contest's implementation plan: the start-of-recording dip in band power, measured two different ways on this participant's own recordings because two of the six contest entries measured it and reported different answers, and both are kept and shown rather than one being picked as right.** New file `ClosedLoopDeployment/startup_bias.py`, ported line for line from the two contest entries' own scratch scripts (`dwell_markov`'s and `nonlin_dyn`'s, neither of which is in this repository — read from the container's read-only copy). Both split this participant's own recorded stretches of band power into an earlier 70% ("training") and a later 30%, ordered by when each stretch started — the same split every contest entry used — and both ask whether the very first reading of a training stretch, or the first several, runs low compared with the rest of that stretch. **They disagree because they define "the first reading" differently, and that difference is stated in the code, in the decision here, and in the sentence printed on the page, rather than resolved in favour of one of them.** One method ("D") first throws out any missing reading from a stretch and then asks for the first reading that is actually there, counting only stretches with more than 20 real readings; the other ("E") asks for the reading at the exact device-clock position — 0 seconds in, 3 seconds in, 6 seconds in, and so on — and simply leaves a stretch out of one particular position if that one reading is missing, with no minimum length at all. Neither method fits anything or simulates anything; both read the identical 3-second recordings `design_rule.py` and `occupancy.py` already read, and are computed fresh on every report rather than stored, the same as `occupancy.py`. Wired into `adapter.report_for_participant` and patched onto the "Adaptive startup delay" row of the parameter card — the same pattern T3 and T4 used for the threshold rows, patching the already-turned-into-plain-data version of the card rather than the underlying objects, which by this point in the code no longer reach the page. `prescription.py` gained a matching `startup_bias_note` field, plus `startup_bias_note()`/`attach_startup_bias()` functions mirroring the two earlier checks'. | Host suite **1162 passed, 2 skipped, 0 failed** (was 1143, +19 new tests); container suite **PASS=631 FAIL=0 LIVE_SKIPPED=6**, both fresh. **The port was checked against the contest's own reported numbers directly, not only against re-derived arithmetic**: fed the identical recorded stretches the contest script itself built (the same participant, the same sensing contact and band, L 1-3+ at 24.5 Hz), this file's two methods reproduce the contest's own printed numbers to every digit — method D: bias -0.10404153099836579 of this participant's own scatter, t=-2.4914058108928567, 82 training stretches (the contest's own words: "0.104 of the scatter low, t=-2.49, n=82"); method E: bias -0.03452435522071541, z=-1.4706266338270466, 221 training stretches (the contest's own words: "0.03... none of them more than 1.5 standard errors from zero"). **On the module's own everyday path — which builds the recorded stretches its own way, a way already in use for T3 and T4 and not changed here — the same band gives close but not identical numbers**: method D, 88 qualifying stretches, bias -0.095, t=-2.40; method E, 237 stretches, bias -0.032, z=-1.42. The cause is disclosed, not smoothed over: this module's everyday path removes a reading from the time record entirely whenever that one reading's band power could not be computed, before it decides where a gap in recording begins and ends — the contest's own scripts kept such a reading's time slot and let the missing-value handling already built into the recording-to-stretches step take care of it instead. Removing it first can turn what would have been one short interruption into what looks like the end of one stretch and the start of another, so the two paths agree closely but not stretch for stretch. This behaviour is not new here — `design_rule.py` (T3) and `occupancy.py` (T4) already build their stretches the same way — so this file matches its own module's established practice rather than the contest's. **On the currently committed band (L 0-2+, Closed-Loop Deployment page)**: method D reads 71 qualifying training stretches, the first reading running 0.045 of the scatter low, not a confident finding (t=-1.04); method E reads 205 stretches, the first reading running 0.007 of the scatter HIGH, essentially flat (z=0.35) — on this band the two methods do not even agree on which direction the first reading leans, which is itself the honest answer this task asked for rather than something to reconcile. **Live proof on RCS08, the committed band, genuinely before and after** (`adapter.py`/`prescription.py` set aside with `git stash -u` for "before", restored for "after"): 49,594 fields before, 49,741 after, 49,594 in common, **0 differing**, 0 only-before, 147 only-after — every added field is the new start-of-recording-dip block or the two `startup_bias_note` sentences (one on the two-mode view, one on the selected-mode view); nothing already on the page moved. Workers not reloaded and not watched in a browser this session. **On screen**: Closed-Loop Deployment page, "Full parameter recommendation" card, on the Adaptive startup delay row. | 124 | **The evidence triangle's current-to-power edge stays a pooled straight-line slope; no peak is fed into it until one is established -- and the PI's standing expectation is that a peak, and the descent past it, will have to be modelled once the data allow it.** Decision 6 of the redesign plan sent the question to a four-worker literature swarm (empirical shape; peak-fitting methods; artifact and saturation; chronic-pain targets) and to a measurement on RCS08's own record; the synthesis is `artifacts/research_2026-09-11_stim_amplitude_vs_lfp_power.md` with the four worker reports beside it. **What the literature shows**: in people, band power FALLS with amplitude and flattens (STN low-beta 4.31 % -> 1.12 % over 0-2.5 mA, Feldmann 2022, n = 10; -21.5 dB at 1.85 mA against -21.0 dB at 2.84 mA, Hammer 2026, 11 hemispheres); a rise-then-fall has been recorded once (GPi, n = 1, near the side-effect level, Cagle 2021); no paper records a single band rising, falling and rising again with amplitude; no human study tests ramp-up against ramp-down; and for ACC, OFC, VPL/VPM or PAG/PVG no amplitude sweep with LFP band power exists in print at all. Sub-harmonics that switch on above ~2.5 mA and amplifier overflow at 4.5-5.3 mA can manufacture a rise-then-fall with no change in the brain, and Percept exposes no blanking control. **What RCS08 shows, measured 2026-09-11** (`_agent_bridge/_probe_tl/probe_amp_power_shape.py`): time-domain route, one intercept per run, 194 contact-band pairs with >= 8 points -- the quadratic beats the straight line at p < 0.05 in **1** (72.5 Hz, outside the sensing range; fitted peak 3.4 mA, bootstrap interval 2.85-25.9 mA); the project's own stored pooled model finds p < 0.05 in **9 of 194 (4.6 %, the chance rate)**; per run the largest power sat at an interior current in 38 % of 388 series, the chance rate for 3-6 currents. The record holds 12-13 points per contact across 3-4 visits, at most 6 currents per run, and the right side mixes three stimulation rates (55, 110, 145 Hz). His answer: *"Accept the pooled slope recommendation ... High likelihood we must model or identify the response peak ... model the post-peak descent ... be prepared to pivot."* The titration protocol that would make a peak estimable (one rate, 0 mA to the side-effect level in 0.5 mA steps, >= 60 s per step, up then down, streaming throughout, an off-stimulation baseline and impedance before and after, the analysis band away from the rate's folded harmonics) is in the synthesis §1 and is open item 30. | Requested directly ("run a Swarm literature research ... do careful descriptive analysis of existing data"). Every literature number above is traced to a worker file and its source; the two claims worker A could not trace to a peer-reviewed paper are marked LOW in the synthesis and are not repeated here. The RCS08 numbers were measured today, not carried forward. No code changed for this decision. | 2026-09-11 |

| 111 | **The reliable-change floor now measures SHORT-GAP noise -- the difference between two ratings filed within one hour of each other under unchanged stimulation settings, same-minute repeat entries removed, computed separately for every pain score -- and the Closed-Loop Deployment page shows it, with a selector for which score to judge against.** The PI's decision after a stress test he asked for, in his words: *"use 1 hour with duplicates removed -- make sure to compute for every different PRO type separately ... the user should be able to identify which PRO value they want to focus on when deciding in the closed-loop deployment."* **WHY THE OLD ESTIMATOR WAS WRONG.** Decision 75 pooled the spread of every rating inside a stretch of unchanged settings. Jacobson & Truax's noise is meant to be the same state measured twice over a short gap, and on this record those stretches run for days: of the 78 NRS stretches that qualified, 75 were longer than 12 hours, the median was 49 hours and the longest 43 days. **Measured before choosing, rather than argued:** within-stretch spread did not grow with stretch length (Spearman ρ = −0.01, p = 0.91), because the patient rates several times a day and the ratings being compared were hours apart whatever the stretch's length -- so the first fix proposed, dropping stretches over 12 hours, would have thrown out 96% of the data (3 stretches left, exactly the minimum degrees of freedom, floor 1.52) to cure a problem the data do not show. The right unit is the gap between two consecutive ratings. **THE SWEEP.** Pairwise SD from consecutive same-stretch ratings, by maximum gap: 1 h 0.30 (23 pairs), 6 h 0.79 (267), 12 h 0.78 (341), 24 h 0.76 (605), no limit 0.78 (671) -- stable from 6 h up. The PI chose one hour because this patient genuinely re-rates within the hour: 23 pairs from 10 stretches, and when they do the answer is almost always identical and never off by more than one point. **Eight of those 23 pairs were two entries at the same minute with the same value** -- one reading recorded twice, and leaving it in pulls the noise toward zero for free. The later entry of any same-minute pair is dropped and the count reported. **WHAT IT NOW READS, live on RCS08:** NRS 15 pairs from 8 stretches (8 same-minute repeats set aside), SD 0.365, threshold **1.01 points**; Overall VAS 15 pairs, SD 5.17, threshold 14.3; MPQ Sum 14 pairs, SD 1.92, threshold 5.3; Left Leg VAS 12 pairs from 7 stretches, SD 11.1, threshold 30.8; Back VAS 12 pairs, SD 6.77, threshold 18.8; Relief 15 pairs, SD 10.3, threshold 28.6. | 107 | **Open item 7's last part is built: every pain score's grid is now computed off the request path, and doing it exposed a silent defect in the shared store that had been there all along.** The grid answers ONE score per request because the score is in its key (decision 38) -- a different score is a different answer, not a cache miss to avoid -- so reading a second score has always cost a whole rebuild, and the page's own background prefetch (decision 82) paid for it up to six times over ON the request path, on a gunicorn worker, while a reader waited. **Built in the same two shapes the stability grid already uses** (decisions 96 and 97): `manage.py precompute_band_sweeps` (`--participant/--all/--metrics/--json/--dry-run/--request-json`), started detached once a grid lands for the OTHER five scores under the settings actually in use, and run daily at the default settings from the same pass `stability_precompute_loop.sh` already makes. The settings travel by the SAME whitelist the stability run uses and for the same two reasons -- they are in the key, and a command line is visible in the process list -- with `SweepMetric`/`LabelMetric` deliberately stripped, since the score is the command's own argument and one carried in as a setting would store one score's grid under another score's name. **THE DEFECT, AND IT WOULD HAVE MADE THE WHOLE FEATURE A NO-OP THAT REPORTED SUCCESS: `CacheStore` kept exactly one entry per participant per kind**, so the six scores -- six entries of one kind for one participant -- evicted each other as they were written. Measured live on RCS08: six grids computed, six writes each reporting `stored: true`, and **one file on disk**; the page then rebuilt a score that had just been stored, in 8.9 s, with nothing on any page or in any log saying why, and every test passing throughout because no test had ever asked what happened to the entry written before last. Fixed in the store itself, where the rule lives: `store.KEEP_NEWEST_BY_KIND` names the three sweep kinds and keeps twelve -- one full set of scores at a reader's settings and one at the daily pass's defaults, about 8 MB a participant at 0.67 MB an entry -- while every kind not named still keeps exactly one, so a 245 MB tile entry cannot quietly start keeping twelve. Unbounded history was rejected for the reason decision 28 gives for Redis: a store that only grows is not a cache. The sweep now removes an entry WHOLE, payload and sidecar grouped by stem, rather than by name prefix. **A fan-out guard was needed and is the other load-bearing piece**: the command builds a grid by calling the ordinary request function, which ends by starting background work -- so without it each of the five runs would start five more, and each would also start a whole-machine stability job of its own, since the stability key carries the score too. One page load would have become a growing tree of real processes with nothing on any page to show it. A private module flag both launchers read holds them off; it cannot arrive over HTTP, and it is read rather than having either launcher's operator switch flipped underneath it, because restoring a value read before a run would quietly undo a change made during it. | Requested directly ("do 26 first, then 7"). **Speed claim and equality proof in the same report, alternating rounds, no tolerance.** Two rounds of served-against-rebuilt for the same score on RCS08, each round deleting the stored entry between the two calls so the second genuinely rebuilt: served **2.9 s and 5.4 s** (and 6.4 s / 4.3 s in a repeat pair) against fresh **10.4 s and 12.0 s** -- **36,401 fields compared each round, 22 differing, every one bookkeeping, 0 scientific**, and the 6 fields present on one side only are named rather than waved at: `stored_utc` on the served answer, and the five scores the fresh request's own launcher listed. That is decision 96's own finding reproduced one launcher later; the markers were removed, the test now switches the feature off, and a re-run leaves none. No file under `Client/src` changed and none needed to: the frontend prefetch of decision 82 still fires, but its requests are now SERVED rather than computed, which is exactly the compute this item asked to move off the page. | 106 | **Open item 26 is closed: the calibrated grid now marks, cell by cell, how much of it was answered from the device's own spectrum -- where the length-of-signal axis means nothing -- and the size of the problem on this record is far larger than the item's own wording suggests.** The item says the device-spectrum route "only affects reports that have none [no voltage trace] in range", which is true and reads as a rare case. **Measured live on RCS08 before anything was built: on the best-covered contact it is most of the data.** R 0-3+ 358 of 451 matched reports (79.4%), L 1-3+ 83 of 174 (47.7%), L 0-3+ 3 of 90, L 0-2+ 5 of 91, R 1-3+ and R 0-2+ none. **The consequence was measured too, not merely inferred from the code**: the correlation's travel from the shortest length of signal to the longest, per band centre, orders exactly with that share -- median 0.037 on R 0-3+, 0.103 on L 1-3+, 0.143 and 0.147 on the two low ones, 0.160 and 0.253 on the two with none. **The contact carrying the most data is the one whose length axis is most frozen, about seven times flatter than the contacts with none**, and nothing on the page said so. **What was built:** `availability.live_lsb_band_medians_by_length` reports one flag per pain report (`from_device_spectrum`); `bravo_service._band_time_sweep_power_by_seconds` returns it from BOTH matching paths, including the older one used for a contact the ceiling table does not cover; and `analytics.band_time_sweep_from_power` counts it WHERE THE CELL IS COMPUTED, so the mark counts exactly the reports that cell used rather than a contact-wide average pasted onto every cell. **The two grids are marked from their own denominators**, because splitting the pain scores discards reports from the area-under-the-curve grid that the correlation grid keeps. The view (`Server/APIs/DataAnalysis.py`) now calls it instead of reaching past into `adapter.report_for_participant`. (2) **THE VIEW'S CATCH-ALL NOW LOGS, which is the root cause of the 2026-09-04 outage.** That handler answered HTTP 200 with `{"available": False, "reason": "deployment report error: ..."}` -- right for the page, catastrophic as the ONLY record: when a module-level import broke the whole package, every request came back through it, nothing was written down, and **it stayed broken for five days and was found by reading code**. `_log.exception` rather than `error`, because the name of the module that would not import exists only in the traceback. (3) **The review found a real defect in decision 100's own `_cache_status_or_reason`**: it returned the explanation under `"reason"`, but the page's `CacheStatusLine.js` renders `status.note` and declares `note` in its propTypes with no `reason` field at all -- so on the one failure that helper exists to survive, the cause would have been present in the JSON and invisible on screen, which is the very failure class decision 100 was fixing, reproduced one level down. (4) The review also found that decision 100's logging guards **walked `report_for_participant` alone**, and `ast.walk` does not descend into a function it merely CALLS -- so `_cache_status_or_reason` sat there with an unlogged handler and both guards passed. The guards now cover a named failure surface of five functions, and **widening them immediately found two more unlogged handlers** in `band_sweep_grid_for_closed_loop` (a failed read of the stored grid, and a per-row failure of the four-valued translation, which the page shows as "not tested" -- indistinguishable from a band that was never tested). Both now log. (5) The key-mismatch guard allowed a handler to file its explanation under `<key>_reason` while never assigning `<key>` itself, which would leave the primary key absent -- the exact "an absent key reads as does not apply" failure the file exists to prevent; it now requires the primary key too. Eight new tests pin the service's own contract, including that a failing report reaches BOTH the caller and the log **with `exc_info`** -- the assertion that would have caught the 2026-09-04 outage on the day it happened -- and that a participant who simply does not exist is NOT logged, since training a reader to ignore the log is its own failure. A ninth pins the wiring, so a future view reaching past `bravo_service` into `adapter` fails a test rather than quietly restoring the old shape. No file under `Client/src` changed. **Still open, and deliberately**: `adapter.py`'s `"response"` kind write passes no `provenance=` (its own comment says this is deliberate, that path having no production caller), and six functions have zero production callers -- decision 82's two plus `direction_consistency.for_band` and `correlation_row_for_band`, `reliable_change.pooled_same_condition_sd`, and `clinic_steps.within_visit_band_scores`. **These are NOT deleted**: decisions 74 and 75 record that several were built as standalone checks to be wired in later, and CLAUDE.md §2 principle 4 warns that things that look dead in this repository are often deliberate.
