| 96 | **The Closed-Loop grid's stability column is now computed in the background as soon as the page's own calibrated grid lands, and it reaches the page.** `band_time_sweep_for_participant` starts `manage.py compute_stability_grid` in its OWN process at the end of the request, on BOTH return paths -- the freshly built grid and the one served from the store -- because a reader is looking at a grid either way and the stability column is what is still missing from it. A separate process rather than a thread, because `stability_grid_for_participant` parallelises by forking and forking is only safe before rpy2's embedded R has started; a gunicorn worker that has answered even one single-candidate band request has already started it, so a thread there would silently take the serial path (8.2 s against 78.3 s on RCS08, for identical answers). Detached with `start_new_session`, so a worker being recycled does not take a half-finished run down with it, and its output goes to a file rather than the parent's pipes. **A run is started only when there is something to do**: skipped when the answer under this exact key is already stored (the ordinary case once a participant has been looked at once), when another run for the same key is inside a 600 s cooldown, when the request carries its own pain reports, when the caller asked for the inline column, and when `STABILITY_GRID_BACKGROUND` is switched off. **The cooldown is a FILE, not a set in this process** -- four gunicorn workers are four independent memories, the same reasoning decision 78 applies to its own held table, so repeated Recompute clicks, a second browser tab and a request landing on another worker all read one marker. Only the sweep's own settings travel to the run, by whitelist (`STABILITY_GRID_SETTING_KEYS`) through a new `--request-json` argument the command filters again on arrival: a command line is visible in the process list, so pain-report rows and the REDCap field map must never reach it, and the settings have to travel at all because they are IN the key. The response carries `stability_background`, saying what happened, so a live check needs no log file. **Two real defects were found by proving this on live data, and neither was visible to a green suite.** (1) `compute_and_store_stability_grid` rebuilt the sweep's key out of the response's echoed `settings_applied` block, which is NOT the block the sweep keys itself on -- the real one carries `match_direction` and `include_cross_setting_stability`, the echoed one carries `match_tolerance_min`, `sweep_metric` and `match_extent_sec_ignored` instead, and it resolved the pain score without the sweep's own `SweepMetric` override. Measured on RCS08: the page asked for key `dc6cec1b8bee68c50ed5ec958404e291f81d1165` while the run it had just started wrote `c02e9aca9e6328765aa4b73ea431558bac2fa246`. Nothing raised, the run succeeded, and the page would have started another whole-machine job on every load forever. **Every test passed through all of it, because each built a key by hand.** Fixed by carrying the sweep's own key in the response (`sweep_key`) and deleting the second derivation outright -- the same two-copies-drift failure this repository already paid for with its two cache stores (decision 30). (2) The launcher was reachable from the unit tests: `test_band_sweep_store`'s bench points the store at a temporary directory and calls the sweep for a made-up participant `u`, and each such call really did start `manage.py compute_stability_grid --participant u`, confirmed by finding the markers those runs wrote under `/tmp`. A background run is now refused while the store is pointed at a caller's own root, and the default is the refusing one -- mirroring `ledger`'s own rule that it records only writes made under the production root, for the same reason: work aimed at a temporary directory is read by nothing. | Requested directly ("wire that shit"), the remaining checkbox of the plan's Phase 4 now that the import blocker of decision 95's session is cleared. **Proven live on RCS08, the whole loop, twice** (the second time after the production-root guard changed the launcher's early path): a page request costs 11.348 s when it builds the grid and 3.2-3.8 s when the store serves it, and starts the run either way; the run finishes 10.8-12.7 s later with 132 of 132 points answered; the key asked for and the key written are identical (`a2954bda86392f6a47cae3fd70eaf794e693339a`); the known case ONE_THREE_LEFT at 12.5 Hz reads `lrt_p` 0.032285156521398184, the same value measured before it was ever stored; a second request reports "the answer for this key is already stored" and starts nothing; and `ClosedLoopDeployment.adapter.band_sweep_grid_for_closed_loop` returns 264 rows ALL carrying a stored answer -- 50 "behaves differently", 204 "cannot tell", 10 "behaves the same" across the correlation and AUC grids. That distribution is the argument for the four-valued answer: a bare `stim_stable` boolean would have called most of those 204 "stable". **Container suite 590 -> 605 passed, 0 failed** (+15 new tests in `test_stability_background_launch.py`, one of which reads `band_time_sweep_for_participant`'s own source and fails if the sweep starts reading a setting the background run does not carry -- the drift that would otherwise leave the page on one key and the answer on another with no symptom but a column that never fills in). **Host suite 993 passed, 42 skipped, 1 failed** -- the known environment mismatch of decisions 84/85, unrelated and unchanged. `STABILITY_GRID_RULE_VERSION` bumped to `v2_stability_grid_sweep_key`, so an entry built under the old key shape is never served as if it carried the new one. | 95 | **The Biomarkers page's "Export full grid to Closed-Loop" button is replaced with a working "Open this grid in Closed-Loop" button, because the export it was written for does not exist and never will.** The old button was permanently disabled behind a check called `exportReady`, which looked for any of three fields on the sweep response -- `closed_loop_export_key`, `exported_to_closed_loop`, `closed_loop_export_ready`. Searching the whole repository with the compiled bundle excluded found all three appear exactly once each, on the single line that READS them; nothing anywhere writes any of them, so the button could never have become enabled for anyone. The three names were a guess, written by Track A while Track D was still in flight, at what Track D would add -- `BiomarkerHeatmapGrids.js`'s own comment said as much. **Track D then landed and decided the opposite** (decision 67, task D1): the calibrated grid was already the whole content of the stored `biomarker_band_sweep` entry, so no new field was added and nothing pushes anything anywhere. Closed-Loop Deployment READS that stored entry as a registered consumer (`adapter.band_sweep_grid_for_closed_loop`) and browses it in its own `BandSweepGridPanel` (task D3). The note under the disabled button therefore told a reader to wait for something that had already shipped, in a different shape, on the same day. **A second, independent blocker sat behind the first**: the click handler in `index.js` was an explicit placeholder that only called `markClosedLoopFamilyStale` and sent no grid anywhere, so even a forced-open gate would have exported nothing. **What replaces it:** `exportReady` is deleted; the button is enabled whenever a grid is on screen (`gridReady = !!corrSw`) and navigates to `/reports/closed-loop/<uid>#cl-grid`; the caption now states plainly that Closed-Loop Deployment reads this same grid. The prop is renamed `onCommitBand` to `onOpenInClosedLoop` because it no longer commits anything, and the `markClosedLoopFamilyStale` call is dropped from this path: browsing changes no stored value, and marking the deployment results out of date stays reserved for actually committing a band, which the older routine's own `onBandCommitted` still does. **The fragment is wired, not decorative:** `#cl-grid` is the anchor already on the grid panel's own Grid item in `ClosedLoopSim/index.js`, but React Router does not scroll to a fragment by itself, so a small effect on that page scrolls to it -- waiting on `bandSweepGrid.loading` to settle rather than firing on mount, because the panel is only a few lines tall while it is still loading and a scroll fired on mount lands where the panel is about to stop being. A ref makes it fire once per arrival, so a later recompute does not yank a reader's scroll position back. **One thing deliberately NOT claimed in the code:** the button's enabled state means a grid is on screen, which in the ordinary case is also a grid in the store, since `band_time_sweep_for_participant` writes its own response back on the way out -- but that write is skipped when no signature could be built, so the comment says so rather than promising a guarantee the code does not make; the Closed-Loop panel already states its own empty case ("no calibrated grid is available for this participant yet"), so the rare miss lands on an explanation instead of on nothing. | Requested directly after the blockage was traced and reported ("YES make that dead button swap/change and wire it"). Frontend rebuilt; the compile reports warnings repository-wide, and of the three touched files only `Biomarkers/index.js` appears among them, at lines 100, 276 and 519 -- all outside this change's single hunk, which `git diff -U0` puts at line 1147, so all three are pre-existing and none was introduced here. `BiomarkerHeatmapGrids.js` and `ClosedLoopSim/index.js` produce no warnings at all. **A correction on this point rather than a silent fix:** an earlier check in this session concluded there were no warnings in any touched file, and that check was run against a build log truncated by the reporting command itself, not against the whole log -- the conclusion was wrong and is restated here from the full output. The served bundle was checked by string rather than by component name (CLAUDE.md §8): the new label "Open this grid in Closed-Loop" and the new caption "Closed-Loop Deployment reads this same grid" are both present in chunk `806.ca7d0c28.chunk.js`; the old label "Export full grid to Closed-Loop" and the old note "Waiting on the Closed-Loop export field" are both absent from every chunk; and `cl-grid` is present in both the Biomarkers chunk and the Closed-Loop chunk, which is what makes the navigation and its scroll target real rather than only written. **Not verified in a browser**: the local instance is not signed in on this session and typing a credential is a hard limit, so the button was not clicked live and the scroll-on-arrival was not watched happen -- disclosed rather than claimed, the same gap the prior session recorded for its own work. | 2026-09-09 |
| 94 | **The band-by-length sweep's outlier rule is replaced, for the six real RCS08 contacts only: instead of 5 median absolute deviations on the log scale computed from the request's own data, a measurement is discarded when it sits above a fixed historical 99.5th-percentile ceiling for that exact (contact, band centre) -- and the discard happens to INDIVIDUAL 3 s pieces of recording BEFORE they are averaged, with the next-closest clean piece taken in place of each one dropped.** His own rule, given verbatim: *"discard the top 0.5% of data using a list of precalculated thresholds that are specifically derived from all historical records... hard coded now and applied to individual windows in future analysis"*, then narrowed twice by him -- first *"forget applying the outlier rule by source... just apply it to the computed LSB values per center per contact pair"* (so one pooled table, not the TD/PSD split decision 52 keeps), then, after seeing the first build measured, the move from cell-level to piece-level: *"If they are excluded before the calculation of individual time lengths, they will apply equally to all time lengths."* **The first build applied the ceiling to the finished cell and was wrong for a measurable reason**: the ceilings are percentiles of INDIVIDUAL 3 s values, so comparing them against a cell that already averaged up to 100 pieces compares a threshold against a much narrower distribution -- measured, it discarded 0.708% of the 1 s row but only 0.300% from the 20 s row up, so "the top 0.5%" meant ten different things down one column, and the same piece of recording got two different verdicts depending on which row a reader looked at. New code: `availability.live_lsb_band_medians_by_length` (eligibility and ownership via the SAME `_pad_windows_in_extent`/`_pad_owned_windows`/`_nearest_pro_idx` helpers `live_lsb_spectrum_match` uses -- `_nearest_pro` was lifted to module level rather than copied -- so only the surviving-piece step differs), `analytics.BAND_SWEEP_LSB_CEILINGS` (the table) and a new `chunk_exclusion` field on every channel's response carrying the counts. **EXCLUSION IS PER BAND, NOT PER WHOLE PIECE, and that was measured rather than assumed**: of 296,157 pieces, 11,208 (3.78%) sit above a ceiling in at least one band but only 4 (0.001%) do in all 22, and a third are over in exactly one band -- so the literal "throw the piece out" would have discarded 3.78% of the record, an eightfold over-exclusion, almost all of it good data. A piece therefore drops out at 8.5 Hz and stays in at 20 Hz. **BACKFILL over drop was his explicit choice**: each rating's eligible pieces are put in one fixed nearest-first order and the value for length N at band c is the median of the first N that are clean AT BAND c, so a cell keeps the sample count its row asks for. Gated to the sweep: only a channel `BAND_SWEEP_LSB_CEILINGS` covers takes this path, so `live_lsb_spectrum_match` and every other panel calling it read exactly what they read before, and any channel the table does not cover (a synthetic test channel, the demo participant, a future contact) falls back to the unchanged MAD rule rather than silently skipping exclusion. `_BAND_SWEEP_RULE_VERSION` bumped to `v6_sweep_per_chunk_ceiling_backfill`. | Units were verified before building, per his explicit "BUT MAKE SURE": `live_lsb_spectrum_match`'s own docstring states the values are "LINEAR LSB (NOT logged) per band center" and they come from the identical `_raw_lsb_cache_cached` tile cache the ceiling table was computed from, so the threshold and the data are the same population, not merely the same convention. Table built once by `_agent_bridge/_sweep_ceiling_export.py` (disposable, gitignored) over RCS08's full history: 582 (channel, centre) groups, 145,894 of 29,103,915 values above the cut (0.5013%), no group under a 20-sample floor. **Proven live on RCS08, field count and difference count, never a tolerance**: 27,882 result fields compared between the old MAD rule and this one, **16,028 differ**; 0 values became absent and 0 came back from absent, so every change is a real number moving because cleaner input reached the same, unchanged calculation. **74 headline rows changed which length of signal they name as best; 0 verdicts changed.** The rule now discards **16,010 of 3,669,094 individual band readings, 0.436% overall** (0.228%-0.656% per contact), against the old rule's 1.90% -- and there is no longer a per-row figure to quote, because the exclusion happens once, before rows exist, which was the point. Backfill confirmed working: of roughly 55,000 cells per contact only 18-216 average fewer pieces than their row asked for, and those are ratings that genuinely had no more clean recording nearby. **Two real defects were found by measuring live data, both invisible to a green suite.** (1) The first version of this path returned no per-length matching summary, silently deleting 420 response fields (`match_stats_by_seconds`, 6 channels x 10 lengths x 7 fields) -- caught only by counting fields on both sides; fixed, and a new test now holds those fields against `live_lsb_spectrum_match`'s own. (2) The "cells left short" counter compared against the requested count rather than against what the rating would have averaged with nothing excluded, so it blamed this rule for ratings that never had enough recording nearby -- reported 1,297 for one contact where the true figure is 154. Two new tests pin the fix directly: one proves two participants' entries survive each other's writes and each other's rebuilds at the `_shared_store`/`_shared_load` level; one proves `evidence_inputs_cached` itself stores under the real participant id, not `.shared.`. Host suite: 992 passed (was 990, +2 new), 42 skipped, 1 failed — the 1 failure is decision 84's own pre-existing, already-explained environment mismatch, confirmed unrelated by name and by re-reading the code before and after this fix touched nothing near it. | 84 | **The host suite (`ClosedLoopDeployment/tests`, `StimOptimizer/tests`, `CacheStore/tests`, `DecodeCommon/tests`) was actually run, for the first time this session, by installing pytest inside the live server container** (`pip install --break-system-packages pytest`, since no virtual-environment tooling — `python3-venv` — was available and pytest exists on neither this sandbox nor, until now, the container) **and running it there with `pytest.ini`'s own command against the live-mounted source. Result: 990 passed, 42 skipped, 1 failed** — none of the 8 files this session touched (decision 83) are implicated in the one failure. **The failure is a genuine environment mismatch, not a regression**: `ClosedLoopDeployment/tests/test_adapter_caching.py::test_no_directory_means_memory_only_and_not_a_failure` monkeypatches the module's own directory override to `None` and asserts a write to the shared cache then reports "nowhere to write" (`False`). That is true only where Django has no `DATASERVER_PATH` configured — the bare `bravo_app` host environment `CLAUDE.md`'s own Environments section describes this suite as meant for. **Inside the live container, Django IS fully configured**, so `store.root_dir(None)` falls through past the monkeypatched override to the real `DATASERVER_PATH`-derived production cache root and the write actually lands (`True`), which the test was never written to expect. Confirmed by reading `CacheStore/store.py`'s `root_dir()` directly, not assumed. **A real, disclosed side effect of running this test inside the container rather than the intended bare environment**: the write it made (`kind="inputs"`, a throwaway signature) triggered `_sweep_superseded`, which — read directly in `store.py` — keys its eviction marker on `".shared."` whenever `participant_uid` is passed as `None`, and `ClosedLoopDeployment.adapter._shared_store` **always** passes `None` for `participant_uid` even though the real participant identity already lives inside the signature tuple itself (`recording_set_signature`'s first element). That means the sweep this test's write triggered could not tell the test's own throwaway entry apart from a real participant's live `"inputs"` cache entry, and the "closed_loop" cache directory was confirmed empty immediately afterward. **This is self-healing, not a correctness or safety issue** — a cache miss on the next real `report_for_participant` call for whichever participant's entry was resident simply rebuilds it fresh (measured elsewhere in this document at 50.04-50.47s cold vs 1.37-1.44s served, decision 41) and re-populates the cache normally; no stored number is wrong, and CacheStore's own design principle (`store.py`'s `root_dir` docstring) is exactly that an unwritable or evicted cache is a miss, never an error. **Flagged, not fixed**: `_sweep_superseded`'s participant-scoping gap (every write of a given kind evicts every OTHER participant's same-kind entry too, in real production, not only under test, because the eviction marker never actually varies by participant for this call site) looks like a genuine, separate finding worth the PI's attention — recorded here rather than acted on, since fixing it was not part of what was asked and touches shared caching infrastructure decision 30 already treats carefully. | Requested directly: "install pytest ... and run it [on the OrbStack server]." Reported honestly rather than declining once run: which suite state is real (990/42/1, not the earlier session's "could not run" disclosure, which this supersedes), why the one failure is not a regression from decision 83's edits, and the real infrastructure side effect the act of testing caused, including the pre-existing scoping gap it surfaced. No source files were changed by this decision. | 2026-09-09 |

Extracted into its own pure function, `_deployment_summary_adaptive_band_gate`, for the same reason. **Two frontend files confirmed fully dead (zero live imports anywhere, and already documented in the codebase's own comments as superseded 2026-09-04) were deleted**: `DeploymentEvidencePanel.js` and `DeploymentVerdictStrip.js`. | 81 | **All four of Agent C's Plotly render-manager findings (the third leg of the same review, `Client/src/graphing-utility/Plotly/index.js` and its 58 real consumer files) are evaluated and NOT fixed.** Four independent research agents verified each finding directly against the code (not the prior review's summary) and weighed cost against benefit; all four returned SKIP. (1) `hovermode: "xy"` (invalid enum, falls back to Plotly's own `"closest"`) is not isolated to the 5 files first named — it is a copy-pasted typo in 10 files — and `"closest"` is the value this same codebase deliberately chooses elsewhere (`BiomarkerAnalytics.js`, `ThreeSourceResponsePanel.js`); the benefit is plausible for 2-3 charts, near-nil for the rest, and unreported by any user in 80 prior decisions. (3) Switching the pain-score dropdown always paid a real recompute because the backend's own stored-result key already (by design, decision 38) includes the metric — a switch is a genuinely different answer, not a bug — but nothing on the frontend remembered an already-computed metric or warmed the others; fixed with a background-prefetch effect (user's explicit choice over eager-blocking or lazy-only) that, once the selected metric's own load settles, sequentially warms the other five metrics' slots, generation-token-guarded so a fast switch or settings change cancels a stale prefetch chain. The pre-existing PRD §3 asymmetric correlation/AUC update rule (a matching-setting change replaces both grids; a binarization-only change replaces only AUC) was preserved exactly, moved into an effect keyed on the cached bundle's own object identity rather than the raw network response. | Requested directly, three user-reported symptoms in one page. Verified live in the user's own real, authenticated browser session against RCS08 (the user signed in themselves, after declining to use offered demo credentials on hard safety grounds — see below): only one Recompute control renders; switching the dropdown across three metrics (Overall VAS, NRS, Left Leg VAS) re-rendered instantly with no loading state; navigating away to Participant Overview and back left the calibrated grid fully populated with the previously-selected metric, rendered instantly with no loading spinner; no console errors on any transition. | 78 | **Decision 22 is amended, at the PI's explicit direction: the pain-report table IS now held between requests, but only for the read-only drill-downs, and the rule is "until the next build", with no clock in it.** His own framing: once the Biomarkers module has loaded and everything is on screen, the report table is held; it is fetched again only when someone presses Recompute on the module, or the page's data is computed from a new load. Implemented as `_PRO_BUILD_CACHE` in `bravo_service.py`: every endpoint that BUILDS something — the module compute (`run_for_participant`, which is what Recompute triggers), the band-by-length grid (`band_time_sweep_for_participant`), the availability timeline, the pain-score preview — still calls `_load_pros` and therefore still fetches fresh exactly as before, and `_load_pros` hands each such fetch to `_remember_pain_reports`, so the held table is always "whatever the most recent real fetch produced". Only the hover preview and the pinned cell panel read it back (`_pain_reports_for_drilldown`). The cache is bypassed entirely for a request carrying its own report rows (`ProcessedPRO`) or an inline field-map override (`RedcapFieldMap`). A second memo, `_RECORDINGS_SETUP_MEMO`, does the same for the recording-derived setup (time-domain and PSD recordings, event and montage PSD blocks, channel list), shared by the grid build and the drill-down so a grid that actually builds leaves the worker ready for the first hover on it — deliberately placed AFTER the grid's stored-response return so a store-served grid still pays nothing for spectra it does not need (decision 38). | **Decision 22's own measurement is not contradicted and is not worked around.** There is still no cheap freshness check: the only content check here (`_pro_table_digest`) hashes the table AFTER it is fetched, so it can dedup a write but can never save the fetch, and REDCap offers no metadata-only "has anything changed" call this codebase can ask. What is bought instead is speed in exchange for a bounded, disclosed staleness: a rating filed while a grid is already on screen does not reach a hover preview until the next build. Every number that is stored, exported or reported still comes from a fresh fetch, because every path that produces one is a build. Proven on RCS08, by counting real fetches with `_load_pros_raw` wrapped: grid build = 1 fetch; six hovers after it = 0 fetches, at 0.024–0.053 s each (against ~2.9 s per hover before any of this work); Recompute = 1 fetch; three hovers after that = 0 fetches. Equality, held-table hover against a forced fresh-fetch hover for the identical cell: 1,335 fields, 0 differences. Equality for the grid build itself, two genuinely fresh builds with the store bypassed on both sides: 27,865 fields, 19 differing, every one a wall-clock `*_seconds` timing field and no scientific value. Container suite 593/0. **Four gunicorn workers means four independent caches; a hover landing on a worker that has not built for this participant simply fetches once and seeds itself, which is self-healing and needs no cross-worker invalidation.** | 2026-09-08 |

## Part 2 — Open items

**Dropped by the PI, do not re-open as work:** generalising beyond RCS08, and patient-information
hygiene as a work item — the latter is an operational note in `OPERATIONS_runbook.md` §9 only.

### Open on the PI

~~1. **Commit identity.** Raised three times, unanswered.~~ **RESOLVED 2026-09-07.** He chose his
   own name and his UCSF address: Prasad Shirvalkar, `prasad.shirvalkar@ucsf.edu`. Applied to
   every commit from that date on; the commits made before it keep the machine identity they were
   made with, and nothing already pushed has been rewritten.
~~2. **The go-ahead to begin the store implementation.**~~ **RESOLVED 2026-09-07.** He gave it, and
   every phase of the store work is now built, tested and pushed.
~~3. **Whether a switching value can sit on a band whose response is peaked**, and whether to fit
   one straight line or something admitting curvature.~~ **RESOLVED 2026-09-08, decision 55.** A
   switching value may sit on a peaked band only on one side of the peak, where one power value
   maps to one current. Every band is tested for curvature first, pooling every stimulation-current
   ladder this participant has, across every visit (in-clinic and at home) rather than one visit
   at a time — accounting for the grouping between visits, not treating pooled points as
   independent, the same discipline decision 17 already applies. When curvature is confirmed and
   fits well, a second straight-line fit is added using only the data from the peak current onward,
   dropping every point before the peak, so a switching value on that stretch has no ambiguity left
   to resolve.
~~4. **Two defects in the shared result-cache contract, which are his files.**~~ **RESOLVED
   2026-09-08, decision 54.** He authorized the fix directly and lifted the do-not-edit
   restriction for it. The double-fetch is very likely already fixed by an existing guard, not
   yet independently confirmed live; the cache is now bounded by a byte budget and by how many
   participants are resident, not by a flat count.
~~5. **Whether the band-by-length sweep becomes the headline statistic.**~~ **RESOLVED
   2026-09-08.** Yes, this is the headline result; the controls on the exploration page (the median
   split and the rest) exist to sanity-check it, not to replace it. **Left open, its own item
   below:** the display needs cleanup — too much text, and the selected contact is not shown
   clearly across every plot — and a separate decision, for whenever that cleanup happens, on
   whether the sweep should run across every patient-reported outcome or only the one currently
   selected.
~~6. **A second sign-off before touching the entangled spectrum-and-correlation pass.**~~
   **RESOLVED 2026-09-08.** Authorized: open it up for the same review the rest of the pipeline
   received, and look for a chance to fold it into the pipeline that already exists separately.
   **The review is complete and found a live correctness gap — decision 57 and open item 9
   below.** No code has been changed; the gap is confirmed but not yet fixed.
~~7~~. **RESOLVED — the display half by decisions 86-93, the every-pain-score half by decision 107
   (2026-09-10).** ~~Clean up and simplify the band-by-length sweep's display** on the Biomarkers exploration page:
   less text, and the selected contact shown clearly on every plot rather than only some. When this
   happens, decide at the same time whether the sweep should run across every patient-reported
   outcome or only the one selected at the top of the page, and how to make the result more

   that would have made the whole thing a no-op reporting success — six scores stored, one file
   left on disk.

30. **A titration session designed so a response peak can be estimated (decision 124).** **Since 2026-09-12 (decision 146) the Stim Optimizer page recommends it, designed from the record, in the card "Titration session to run next"; the code side waits on the data as before.** On RCS08
    today no band in 8-30 Hz shows an established bend, and the record cannot support one: 12-13
    points per contact, at most 6 currents per run, three stimulation rates mixed on the right. What
    would make a peak estimable, from the research synthesis §1: one session per side per sensing
    contact, at ONE stimulation rate, 0 mA to the side-effect level in 0.5 mA steps, at least 60 s
    per step, up and then down, streaming throughout, an off-stimulation baseline and an impedance
    reading before and after, the analysis band kept away from |250 - rate| and the rate's 1/2, 1/4
    and 3/4 sub-harmonics. A protocol and clinic decision, not a code change; the code side (a
    quadratic with one intercept per visit, a curvature test first, a Fieller or bootstrap interval
    on the vertex, decision 55's second fit past the peak) is designed in the synthesis §5 and waits
    on the data.

~~31~~. **RESOLVED 2026-09-16, decision 180 -- option (b): the record owns the pair.** What follows is the question as put.
    **Threshold placement has two candidate rules and one owner is needed; decision 139 does not
    interfere with the Kalman design rule, but the capture rule it left in place does not USE it.**
    Asked by the PI 2026-09-15 ("address concern that 139 may interfere with statespace/kalman
    model for thresholds"). Checked in the code: the two threshold VALUES on the Closed-Loop page's
    parameter card are still the device's own capture rule (D24: the upper threshold is the mean band
    power measured at the LOWER current, the lower threshold the mean at the HIGHER current;
    `prescription.py`). Decision 139 changed only the two D26 verdict sentences beside them (they read
    the pooled slope and warn instead of block); it moved no value. The Kalman design rule (T3,
    decision 152) and the occupancy check (T4, decision 153) READ that pair and annotate it -- "the
    stored pair is +-2.6; the rule needs +-5 at 3 s / 30 s"; "2.0% of readings land between the two,
    centred 12.4 units above the median" -- and neither writes a threshold. So there is no
    interference: 139, T3 and T4 are all commentary on a pair the capture rule owns. The concern is
    real in the other direction: the pair the page recommends is the capture pair, and on the
    committed band that pair FAILS both of the checks printed under it. Two ways out, his call:
    (a) keep the device's capture rule as the value and the Kalman/occupancy lines as warnings, as
    today; (b) place the pair from the record instead -- centre on the participant's own median
    reading at the recommended averaging (T4) and separate by the Kalman rule's minimum at the
    recommended timing (T3), with the capture pair shown beside it as what the tablet would have
    produced. (b) is what decision 150's contest synthesis recommended ("thresholds to be separated
    ... and centred on the median"); it changes the two numbers a clinician types, so it is not made
    here. If (b): one function in `ClosedLoopDeployment` owning the pair, the D26 checks re-read
    against it, T3/T4 turned from warnings into the inputs, and a live before/after on both bands.

### Open engineering, not blocked on anyone

~~26~~. **RESOLVED 2026-09-10, decision 106.** ~~The length-of-signal axis of the calibrated grid means nothing for a cell served by the
    device's own spectrum, and the page does not say so.** Found 2026-09-09 by reading
    `availability.live_lsb_spectrum_match` directly: the length-of-signal value (`td_quantity_s`)
    is read ONLY in the voltage-trace block (lines ~1911-2017) and never anywhere in the
    device-spectrum block (lines ~2019-2057), which uses the match-tolerance setting alone. So for
    a pain report with no voltage trace in range, the "5 m" row and the "3 s" row hold the
    identical number computed from the identical events. The voltage trace is preferred whenever
    any is eligible, so this only affects reports that have none — but nothing on the grid marks
    which cells those are, and a reader comparing rows down such a column is reading a constant as
    if it were a trend. Options: mark those cells, report the count per contact, or state the
    limitation in the drawer. **A display and reporting decision, his call, not a defect to fix
    silently.** **He chose to mark the cells, and all three options were built rather than one**:
    a per-cell dash sized by that cell's own share, the count reported per contact pair, and the
    limitation stated in the drawer. **The measurement decision 106 records changes how this item
    should be read**: "this only affects reports that have none" is true but sounds rare, and on
    this record it is 358 of 451 matched reports on the best-covered contact, whose correlation
    then travels 0.037 across the entire length axis against 0.160-0.253 where none are affected.

    SUBDIRECTORIES are untracked and unignored, as are the `.pkl`/`.npy`/`.txt`/`.svg` data

10. **REWRITTEN 2026-09-10 (decision 109), because the item as first written could not be acted
    on: it describes a figure that does not exist in this platform, and its counts do not
    reproduce.** The original text is struck below and kept, per this log's convention.

    **What is actually true.** There is **no 110 Hz heat map anywhere in the platform** — no page
    renders one. The only 110 Hz artifact is the pre-registration itself,
    `BRAVO/modules/ClosedLoopDeployment/PREREG_RCS08_110Hz.json`, and **no page reads that file and
    no code imports the reader written for it** (`registry.py` has zero importers). It is a written
    commitment and nothing more. The figure may only ever have existed in a document outside this
    repository.

    **The pre-registration is about a different electrode than the item's replacement.** Its two
    candidates are ZERO_THREE_RIGHT (the electrode on the RIGHT) paired with left-side stimulation
    current, and paired with right-side current. The item's proposed replacement is the left 1-3
    contact. Those are different things, and the item never said which one the figure showed.

    **The pre-registration has not been run and cannot be yet.** Its stopping rule commits to ONE
    future prospective 110 Hz ladder session on ZERO_THREE_RIGHT — steps of at least 105 s, a span
    of at least 3.0 mA, rate, pulse width and contacts fixed, streaming throughout — analysed once,
    after that session is ingested, with no interim look. It also already records that the result
    is not significant today: the right pairing reached p = 0.0470 at one cluster-forming threshold
    and 0.1614, 0.1659 and 0.1524 at the other three, and the left pairing never went below 0.05.
    Its own note says one-of-four below alpha is selection. **Nothing here is waiting on code.**

    **Measured 2026-09-10 on the left 1-3 sensing pair, which is what the replacement named.**
    Counting days with a recording while the rate was 110 Hz gives **43 days**, of which 6 have the
    right stimulator at exactly 0.0 mA — and one of those six is 2025-08-21, where **the LEFT
    stimulator was also at 0.0 mA**, so that day carries no left-side stimulation at all rather than
    the two-sides-in-lockstep the original text describes. That leaves **five genuinely left-only
    days — 6, 9, 14, 16 September and 6 October 2025 — each with exactly one recording and a single
    current** (2.5 mA once, 4.0 mA four times). **There is no amplitude ladder on any of them**, so
    there is nothing to plot power against and "the same 15 steps" cannot be assembled. Cross-
    checked two ways that agree: matching each recording to the setting in force at its start, and
    the project's own `exposure_epochs`.

    **What remains genuinely unknown, and is NOT claimed either way**: which definition of "day"
    and "step" the original text used, whether the p = 0.064 result rests on data this check did
    not find, and what the heat map plotted. Anyone reopening this must re-measure first.

    ~~**The pre-registered 110 Hz heat map should be replaced, not re-rendered.** On its visit the two

### Notes for the clinic — NOT open items

**Moved out of the open list on 2026-09-10 at the PI's direction: "only code changes should be
open items."** These describe what a future visit would need to record. Nothing in the code is
waiting on them and nothing in the code can fix them.

- **55 Hz has almost no data that can be attributed to one side, on the left electrode.** Of six
  visit days with 55 Hz recordings there, exactly one has the right stimulator held at zero. There
  is no second day to confirm against, so nothing at 55 Hz can be called established until there is.
- **The device never computes its own spectrum while the current is being stepped up.** It does
  that only on a patient button press or during a contact survey with stimulation off. So that
  source of data is empty for every current ladder. Populating it means a button press at each held
  setting during the visit.

### Resolved by this consolidation — do not re-open

- **"Reading recordings off disk is the largest cost and should be optimised next."** Two different
  requests were being compared. On the whole Biomarker page, reading and un-pickling all 2,007
  stored files is 2.89 s of a 60.4 s request, 4.8 percent, because loading is already spread over
  sixteen threads. **The dominant cost is re-derivation: 72,425,865 channel-name normalisations per
  request, 99.87 percent of them from one line, worth 9.51 s.** The 21.1 percent figure is real and
  describes the warm band-by-length sweep instead.
- **"The module README needs three edits."** Its content is superseded by this document set, and the
  three edits are incorporated: the closed-loop module's use of the calibrated route, the removal of
  a screening gate that exists nowhere in the code, and the section that deliberately ignores the
  length-of-signal slider.
- **"Matching pain ratings to brain-signal chunks is probably the slowest step of Recompute, and the
  place to look for a vectorization win."** Measured live on RCS08, twice, back to back: matching
  cost 0.09-0.74 s of a 40-43 s Recompute request, 0.2-1.7 percent, and is already array-wide math
  with no per-pair loop (the `958cc89` vectorization already covers it). **The two costs that
  actually dominate have nothing to do with matching**: a 1,000-shuffle randomization test repeated
  nine times inside the older notebook-derived routine, 33.6-36.7 percent, and a separate scan
  across about 90 candidate bands on 6 sensing-contact pairs, 22.0-22.8 percent. There is no
  performance case for touching the matching step further.


### Then

**Design the closed-loop simulation module against the `BandCandidate` contract.**

## Part 3 — Commit lineage

**Read from `git log`, not from recollection.** These are pointers into history; none of them is the
current head unless said so.

### The merged history into the default branch `v3.1.0`

| Pull request | Commit | What it carried |

Earlier branch points worth keeping: `f915257` (the modality-sensitive conversion, the threshold-mode
guard), `e9d7a80` (the 8.8 Hz cut date), `184ea74` and `fa2c416` (removing the dead Welch route and
its constant), `09798f7` (the deployment fallback rebuilt off the raw voltage trace),
`e8a0d3f` (a rebuild dropping a retired tier label). `cd09845` and `f6849c4` are stale heads quoted
in archived documents.

### Source-of-record documents that are not repository files

- **The design ledger** `DESIGN_biomarker_pipeline_v2.md`, revision 13 — artifact
  `f9b3d791-7e95-44bb-bd81-8aebcf9e1b3b`, latest version
  `c7bf4b85-4867-4e3a-b8de-2ba900d1fd9b`. A duplicate record `917ae0bd-7f0a-4fd5-af3d-8a00d4446936`
  exists, so a search returns two rows. An archived index cited `bab71722-0293-453e-9d21-36b77a26cbac`,
  which is superseded.
- **The four-lens audit of record** `closedloop_audit_report.md`, version
  `e3a12136-e0e1-4fff-b95f-baa42d0a0a46` — **the source for the remaining audit backlog in open
  item 15.** The medium and low decision sheets are in `docs/archive/2026-09-07/`.
- **The cache inventory** — `CACHE_MAP.md`, `cache_map.png`, `cache_map.excalidraw`,
  `cache_map.html` and `build_cache_map.py`, saved as artifacts and also present in the untracked
  `cl_docs/` folder. The PI's own edited flowchart is the artifact `newcachemap.excalidraw`.

### Environments

- **`bravo_app`** — Python 3.11 on the host, for decoding free of Django and pure-function checks.
- **`rocqa`** — plotting, with the headless-browser picture export **broken in this sandbox**.
- **The live container** — Python 3.12.3, rpy2 3.5.15, pymer4 0.8.2, pandas 2.2.3, sklearn 1.5.2.
- **The device exported files** are at the shared-drive grant `…/PNL/RCS008 jsons`, whose file
  names carry real patient names; keep that folder out of the repository.
